<meta>This page marks the beginning of the Preface (Vorrede). The signature ")( 2" indicates this is the second leaf of the introductory matter. I have preserved the early modern distinction between "Chymie" and "Alchymie" by using the period-appropriate term "Chymistry" for the former.</meta>

<image-desc>An ornamental headpiece featuring a central crown, floral sprigs, and two spiraling pillars entwined with vines, framed by decorative fan motifs on either end.</image-desc>

# Preface.

<image-desc>A large decorative drop cap 'D' enclosed within an ornate frame of scrolling foliage and topped with a small crown.</image-desc>

Chymistry <term>Chymistry: a historical term used before the 18th century to describe the unified field of chemical science and alchemical speculation</term>, one of the noblest sciences, is divided into many parts, among which Alchemy is the crown; for it occupies itself with the most excellent arts, which for various reasons since antiquity have been hidden from the eyes of the ungodly <note>original: "Gottlosen"; in this context, it refers to those considered spiritually or morally unworthy of divine secrets</note> and practiced in secret as much as possible by those who possess them. This art, Alchemy, had an uncommonly large number of seekers because of the wealth and health it promised, and yet it would allow itself to be found in its treasures by only a few.

<summary>This preface introduces Chymistry as a noble science and defines Alchemy as its "crown," a secretive art offering health and wealth only to a select few.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, chymistry, secret knowledge, health, wealth, preface</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the Preface. The text begins with the suffix of the verb from the previous page, "lassen" (to allow), completing the thought that the Art of Alchemy allowed itself to be found by only a few.</meta>

-># Preface.<-

...allow. One sought her <note>In alchemical literature, "The Art" (Alchemy) is frequently personified as a woman—a Virgin or a Queen—representing pure, hidden wisdom.</note> on these paths, and another on others. Many found something, but most came away empty-handed. These latter ones became partly despisers of the **Art**, and partly deceivers. They violated the honor of this **Virgin** in various ways, and brought it about—some through their writings and others through fraud—that this **Virgin**, despite all the testimonies bestowed upon her by so many wise men from ancient times, was declared by most to be a harlot who, through her caresses, robs one of honor, property, and lifeblood <note>original: "Gut und Blut"; a common idiomatic expression for one's entire worldly existence and life</note>. Consequently, no further distinction was made among her suitors <term>Suitors: original "Liebhaber," referring to devoted students or practitioners of the alchemical art</term>. Anyone who allowed it to be noticed that they paid any attention to an **Art** famous in former times was immediately deemed, if not a deceiver, then certainly a **Fool**. This disgrace pained the suitors of this **Virgin**. Some among them 

<meta>catchword: bore</meta>

<summary>This page describes how failed practitioners and frauds tarnished the reputation of Alchemy, turning a once-venerated "Virgin" of wisdom into a social pariah and branding its followers as fools.</summary>
<keywords>reputation of alchemy, personification of art, virgin vs harlot, fraud in alchemy, historical prejudice</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the Preface. The previous page ended with "Some among them," and the catchword "bore" (trugen) leads directly into the first line of this page.</meta>

-># Preface.<-

)( 3

bore histories of <term>Projection: the process of casting a small amount of the "Philosopher's Stone" or alchemical powder onto molten base metal to transmute it into gold or silver</term> and healing together in order to place the truth of the **Art** before one's eyes. However, opponents arose who strove to overturn the foundations of historical truth and to prove that Alchemy had been nothing but fraud at all times and among all people. These irrational doubters threw the baby out with the bathwater <note>original: "gossen das Kind mit dem Bad aus"; a classic German idiom meaning to lose the good while trying to get rid of the bad</note>. They flattered themselves with a sense of cleverness in the midst of their own folly. They passed judgment on a matter that they did not understand. Their wit was like that displayed by the despisers of **Holy Scripture**, which maintains its prestige among those of understanding, even though it is distorted, despised, and rejected by the uncomprehending—who only take note of the fruits of ignorance and lack of understanding that divide Christians, and who remain far removed from a true understanding,

<meta>catchword: also</meta>

<summary>This page defends alchemy against skeptics who dismiss the entire field as fraud, comparing their irrational rejection of alchemical history to the way critics unfairly dismiss Holy Scripture.</summary>
<keywords>defense of alchemy, history of projection, alchemical healing, skeptics, Holy Scripture, religious analogy, historical truth</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the Preface. The previous page ended with a discussion of those far removed from true understanding, and the catchword "also" (thus/so) leads into the opening phrase regarding their lack of desire to seek the truth.</meta>

-># Preface.<-

and thus also did not wish to seek such a thing. Now a reasonable doubter steps forward. He does not doubt the truth of the **Art** <term>Art: The "Hermetic Art" or "Great Art," a common historical term for alchemy</term>, but he does doubt the correctness of many writings and processes that concern the **Art**. He prides himself on having judged the truth of the **Art** through the precise agreement of the writings of many true **Possessors** <term>Possessor: An adept or master who has successfully created the Philosopher's Stone</term>, who—however strange their words may sound in the ears of the ignorant—have demonstrated a very singular shrewdness. He is not himself a **Possessor** of the **Art**, yet he is sufficiently instructed in it that he finds himself convinced of its truth. He seeks neither wealth nor honor from the **Art**, but rather the wisdom and understanding that accompany it. He possesses in Christ a better **Stone** <note>original: "Stein"; the author is making a theological comparison between the alchemical Philosopher's Stone and Christ as the "spiritual cornerstone"</note> than the **Art** ever brought to light. He loves the **Art** because he perceives that many

<meta>catchword: among</meta>

<summary>The author introduces the perspective of a "reasonable doubter" who, while not an alchemist himself, believes in the Art's fundamental truth based on the consistency of master alchemists' writings, valuing wisdom over material gold.</summary>
<keywords>reasonable doubter, truth of the Art, alchemical writings, Possessors, wisdom vs wealth, Christ as the Stone, alchemy and faith</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the characterization of the "reasonable doubter" introduced in the Preface. It picks up mid-sentence, describing the moral character of true alchemists and the author's intent to defend their legacy against modern skepticism.</meta>

-># Preface.<-
)( 4

among their devotees loved God in Christ and their neighbors as themselves. He cannot look with indifferent eyes upon the fact that, because of the many **Fraudsters**, people wish to declare wise and pious men as fools and cheats after their death.

He endeavors, through a collection of old **Processes** <term>Processes: Step-by-step laboratory instructions or recipes for alchemical transformations</term>, to show that, even if—as many believe—no **Possessor** <term>Possessor: An adept or master who has successfully created the Philosopher's Stone</term> can be found in our own time, there were nonetheless many in past times whom God permitted to see the truth of the **Art** <term>Art: The Hermetic Art or "Great Art" of alchemy</term> with their own eyes.

He believes that among the **Processes** in this collection, there are many which, according to the experience of **Connoisseurs** <note>original: "Kenner"; those with deep, practical knowledge of chemical operations</note>, deserve to be tested through experiment.

He does not wish to incite people to **Laboring** <term>Laboring: Engaging in hands-on chemical or alchemical work in a laboratory</term>; rather, he wishes to hold back as many as make money and worldly goods their primary goal. He wants to put a stop to the **Fraudsters** and **Process-peddlers** <note>original: "Proceßkrämern"; those who sold fake or unverified alchemical recipes for profit</note>

<meta>catchword: do (completing the phrase "put a stop to")</meta>

<summary>The author argues that while modern alchemists may be scarce, historical records of "Possessors" prove the Art's validity. He presents this collection of processes to preserve the reputation of pious ancestors and to discourage those seeking only financial gain.</summary>
<keywords>defense of alchemy, historical adepts, alchemical processes, moral integrity, laboratory work, anti-fraud, greed in alchemy</keywords>

-># Preface.<-

do, and promote the temporal welfare of one's neighbor. He praises the writings which lead to the best path, for the understanding of which he should also provide a permissible guide.

He acknowledges that many possessors of the art <term>Possessors of the Art: Adepts who have successfully achieved the "Great Work" or created the Philosopher's Stone</term>, like other men, possessed many weaknesses of the intellect and will, and that only a few among them were as good theologians <note>original Latin: "Theologi"</note> as they were chemists <note>original Latin: "Chymici"</note>, since theology <note>original: "Theologie"</note> and chemistry <note>original: "Chymie"</note> must be linked for the proper use of the Art <term>Art: The Hermetic or Alchemical Art</term>. 

He defends no liars or frauds, but rather men who demonstrate in their writings a keen intellect, a deep insight into the secrets of nature, and a special love toward **God** and their neighbor. Among other things which seem unbelievable to the uninitiated <note>original: "Idioten"; in this historical context, referring to the "unlearned" or those without specialized knowledge of the craft</note> regarding the Art, is the variety of colors which [are produced] through

He highlights that the spectacular color transformations in the work are often dismissed as impossible by those who do not understand the craft.</summary>

)( 5

the fire come to light, is not the least among them, as it constitutes a part of the wondrous works placed by **God** into nature. We ourselves have, through saltpeter <note>A naturally occurring mineral (potassium nitrate) essential in alchemy and the making of gunpowder</note> and woman's milk <note>original: "Frauenmilch"; literally human milk, which was occasionally used in early modern laboratory experiments and medicinal recipes</note>, discovered a salt which becomes a black liquid <term>Liquor: A term used in historical chemistry for a liquid substance or solution</term> when mixed with water. This liquid, to the greatest delight of the eyes, allows all the colors of the world to be seen in the most beautiful metallic luster in the following way:

A small amount of this liquid is poured into a white glass vessel that has a neck a span <note>An old unit of measure based on the width of an expanded hand, approximately 9 inches</note> long and a belly <note>The rounded, bulbous part of a flask</note>, and which can be sealed at the top against spilling and evaporation. It is then shaken one or several times by hand; in this way, from a portion of the liquid, clear and sometimes very large bubbles are formed—depending on whether the glass is large or small—which, down on the surface of the blackness—

<meta>This page continues the description of the chemical experiment from the previous page, where saltpeter and milk are mixed to form a black liquid. The text picks up mid-sentence, describing the behavior of the bubbles formed after shaking the glass vessel.</meta>

-># Preface.<-

-ness <note>Continuing from "blackness" on the previous page</note> to rest, representing upward arches and other figures, though in the neck at the top, they appear as circles standing one above the other. When these bubbles—which cling to the glass with **golden-yellow threads**—have stood in their white color for a short time in the light of the window or the sun, one sees colors emerge within them with a metallic luster. These colors, which could not be seen more beautifully or subtly in nature, appear in the most perfect proportions <note>Likely referring to the harmonious mathematical ratios of the color spectrum</note>, just as in a rainbow. In the circular bubbles within the neck, one ring is red, another yellow, the third green, the fourth blue, and so on toward the center, revealing a diverse array of colors. Once these colors have played <note>original: "gespielet"; an evocative term describing the shimmering or shifting movement of light and color</note> for some time, the tincture <term>Tincture: In early chemistry and alchemy, an essential liquid or spirit, often highly colored, believed to contain the medicinal or transformative power of a substance</term> within the white bubble contracts, and the white color gradually emerges from the sides of the yellow thread, spreading all around.

-># Preface.<-

This contraction occurs amidst a constant changing of the colors. When the glass is taken in hand and tilted to this or that side, the **tincture-matter** <term>Tincture-matter: The essential, color-bearing substance within an alchemical or chemical mixture, believed to contain its most potent properties</term> swims across amidst a mixing of the circles, like a **tulip** with a short stem. When the glass is set down again, the colors are seen to be mixed. After this **color-spectacle** <note>original: "Farben-Lustspiel"; literally a "color comedy" or play, suggesting a delightful and intentional theatrical display of light</note> of a **philosophical garden** <note>A "philosophical garden" is a common alchemical metaphor for a successful chemical process where crystalline or colorful structures appear to "grow" within the vessel, mimicking biological life</note> has lasted for three to four hours, and the colors have gradually contracted toward the center of the bubble to the size of a lentil, the white bubble then disappears. The yellow threads, however, remain hanging on the sides for twenty-four hours and longer in the form of a **burnt-out gold thread**, unless one wishes to stage a new **theater of colors** <note>original: "Farben-Theatrum"; continuing the metaphor of the experiment as a staged performance for the observer</note> by shaking the glass in the meantime, as

<keywords>alchemy, color-spectacle, philosophical garden, tincture-matter, gold thread, chemical theater, visual observation, tulip</keywords>

...which can happen as often as one wishes, and is repeated until the figures of the *theater* <note>original: "Theatri"</note> please the observer. Regarding the bubbles beneath the neck of the glass, which for the most part form arches; their colors, as with the circular bubbles, are initially in an exact proportion of layers. However, they change bit by bit. Toward their end, as a special delight to the eyes, they drift past one another in a constant motion, like the clouds in the sky, and at the very last, a multitude of serpentine and black lines rise up out of the darkness, which draw the **color-matter** <term>Color-matter: The essential substance responsible for the vibrant hues in the experiment, often called "tincture" in alchemical texts</term> down from the arched bubbles like a curtain, thus bringing an end to the **spectacle** <note>original: "Lustspiel"; literally a "comedy" or "play," implying a staged and pleasing performance</note> from below. From this general description of this **color**-*comedy* <note>original: "Farben-Comoedie"</note>, in which the colors may also be seen at night by a light, it is easy to de-

In this invention, you see a *niter* <note>original: "Nitrum"; saltpeter, which many alchemists believed was a universal spirit from the air</note>, a fruit of the *niter*, of which *Sendivogius* <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and physician</note> among other *philosophers by fire* <note>original: "Philosophis per ignem"; a term for alchemists who used the furnace to study nature</note> testifies; it comes down to us from above and contains within itself the colors of all visible things. Just as on the wet path, so too on the dry path of the philosophers are many kinds of works found, and our stone is salt, of whatever kind it may be. For salt is the *quintessence* <note>original: "Quintaessenz"; the purest, fifth element that binds the other four together</note> of all things, and within it, spirit, soul, and body are joined together. Only **God** can bring all things back into a salt, and this salt into a spiritual essence, or one invisible to the human eye, which has come from **God** through **Christ**, and from which all visible and invisible

-># Preface.<-

<meta>catchword: things</meta>

things became at the beginning of things. Once you have understood the transformation described in **1 Corinthians 15** <note>original: "1 Cor. 15"; a biblical passage focused on the resurrection and the transformation of the physical body into a spiritual one</note> in its full scope, through **Christ**, the **Lord** from heaven, then you will not hold me in any ill suspicion. What a wise man can achieve through fire—by making the visible invisible and the invisible visible—**God** can also do through **Christ**, the center of **God's** wisdom. The true knowledge of **Christ** is a master key to understanding the revealed mysteries of **God**. 

The artist <note>original: "Künstler"; a common term for an alchemical adept or practitioner of the "Great Work"</note> brings all bodies back into a water, whose parts he separates by fire in order to set aside what is impure and to bring the pure parts together into one single thing for wonderful effects. Just as the Jews and many Christians have, the alchemists have also until now awaited Elias <note>referring to "Elias Artista," a messianic figure in alchemical lore predicted to appear at the end of days to reveal the final secrets of nature</note>, through whom the veil of prejudices from their.

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Preface," completing the sentence from the previous page regarding the removal of the "veil of prejudices."</meta>

-># Preface.<-

<meta>catchword: their</meta>

their eyes might be removed. We also believe, according to the Scriptures, that **Elias** <note>Elias Artista, a legendary figure in alchemy predicted to appear at the end of time to reveal all hidden secrets of nature</note> will come, but not as a physical person. Rather, he will come in spirit and power—not in a single man as in the ancient days of **John the Baptist**, but in many thousands of wise men. These men shall arise among all nations and sweep away the old filth of prejudices and errors. **Revelation 11, 12.** 

Whether this time has already appeared, we do not wish to prove here. We say only this: we have sufficient reason to believe that the time has come in which a great understanding of divine and natural mysteries will be granted by **God**. We do not wish to break the **Seal of Hermes** <note>original: "Sigillum Hermetis"; this refers to the tradition of keeping alchemical secrets "hermetically sealed" from the unworthy</note>. We know how far its reach extends. However, anyone who stretches it further is unwise, even if he were the possessor of many <term>Tinctures</term> <note>original: "Tincturen"; in alchemy, these are potent liquid preparations, such as the Elixir of Life or the liquid form of the Philosopher's Stone, used for transformation or healing</note>. Such...

<meta>catchword: Such</meta>

<summary>The author explains that the prophesied "Elias" is not a single person but a collective spiritual awakening of wise men who will clear away historical errors. He hints that an era of profound divine and natural revelation has begun, though he maintains the necessity of alchemical secrecy.</summary>
<keywords>Elias Artista, John the Baptist, Revelation, Seal of Hermes, alchemical tinctures, divine mysteries, secrecy, spiritual enlightenment</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the "Preface" from the previous page, completing the sentence describing those worthy of hermetic knowledge.</meta>

-># Preface.<-

similar arts belong to wise men who are enemies of all lies from the heart and who demonstrate a true desire to please **GOD** in all matters. Anyone who withholds such an art from such a man is unworthy of possessing it and will one day have to give an account to **GOD**. 

Just as ordinary wealth does not free one from follies, but rather entangles one in them—so long as one is not yet rich in understanding and will in **Christ**—likewise, the extraordinary wealth of a <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur"; the alchemical Elixir or Philosopher's Stone in liquid form, capable of transmuting metals or healing the body</note> does not exclude those from follies who are still far behind in understanding and virtue. Such possessors do not apply their treasure toward the purpose for which it was given by **GOD**. They keep it hidden and do not perform the good works to which love for one's neighbor, according to the instruction of the 

<meta>catchword: Scrip-</meta>

<summary>The author argues that alchemical secrets should only be shared with virtuous men and warns that possessing the "Tincture" without spiritual maturity leads to folly. He emphasizes that this divine treasure is intended for performing good works and helping one's neighbor.</summary>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Preface" from the previous page, completing the sentence regarding the obligations set forth by the "writings of the wise."</meta>

-># Preface.<-
->)( )(<-

writings of the wise. They travel for their own amusement here and there, and rarely think of the need of the poor. They keep more of their wealth than they and their kin—according to the stations in life in which **GOD** placed them—will ever have need of. They practice no spiritual increase <note>original: "Wucher"; here used in the biblical sense of "usury" or "interest," referring to the Parable of the Talents where one is expected to grow the gifts God has provided</note> with their temporal talent, but rather bury it, insofar as they, out of greed and envy, hide their science <note>original: "Wissenschaft"; in this 18th-century context, this refers specifically to the secret knowledge of alchemy or the hermetic arts</note> even from those who, by all signs, would be faithful stewards of such a treasure. It is true that no one can look into the heart of another as **GOD** can.

Yet it is also certain that the children of **GOD** quickly learn to know one another by their works. It is true that one should be mindful of the preservation of one's freedom and life. It is also certain, however, that one should [perform] the works of love with such prudence—

<meta>catchword: -ness (completing the word "prudence")</meta>

<summary>The author criticizes those who hoard alchemical knowledge and wealth for selfish pleasure, failing to act as "faithful stewards" for the poor. He argues that while only God knows the heart, a person's spiritual character is revealed through their charitable works.</summary>

The <term>processes</term> <note>original: "Processe"; in this context, these are chemical recipes, laboratory instructions, or formulas for metallurgical transformations</note> contained in this collection are almost all taken from manuscripts which are one hundred to four hundred years old, and for the most part written in the Latin language <note>Latin was the standard language for scientific and alchemical scholarship throughout Europe until the early modern period</note>. It took great effort to bring these illegible writings, with their unusual abbreviations and signs, to a proper understanding. Since more honesty was found among the ancient alchemists than among many of those today, these old manuscripts still hold a certain preference among connoisseurs. However, that there were also <term>sophists</term> <note>original: "Sophisten"; a term used in alchemy to describe fraudulent practitioners or "puffers" who used deceptive rhetoric and false chemical demonstrations to trick the unwary</note> among the ancient alchemists is testified in particular by *Bernhardus Trevisanus* <note>Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490) was a famous Italian alchemist who allegedly spent his life and fortune searching for the Philosopher's Stone; he often wrote critiques of the many frauds and "sophists" he encountered during his travels</note>. # Preface.

today's scholars, by making a point of pride out of their ignorance in <term>alchemy</term> <note>original: "Alchymie"; the historical art of transmuting substances and seeking spiritual or physical perfection</note>, could think themselves wiser than these and so many other scholars of past times. Whoever is an expert in a science is one who can judge it. To all others, the saying: *Let the shoemaker stick to his last* <note>original: "Ne sutor ultra crepidam"; a Latin proverb warning people not to offer opinions outside their own field of expertise</note> is held against them. Whoever stands on the shoulders of the ancient scholars, and thus looks out beyond them, does not fall to his knees before them, but before the truth, as often as he finds it within their work. Scripture and Reason are the two main sources of truth. Whatever has not flowed from these is no sooner said than it is already rejected. This is how all true philosophers are minded, as they collectively assign the proper value to wisdom. To these [philosophers], the author commends himself with all love and loyalty, just as little as he will care about being despised by low-minded souls.

...has been revealed. Since the same <note>Referring back to Divine Wisdom or Nature mentioned on the previous page.</note> has left us so many beautiful writings, these are a means through which **God** wishes to impart the understanding of this greatest mystery of nature to many. Whoever seeks this understanding with impure intentions, and not with those that bring a true advantage over other men, should not flatter himself that he will obtain it from **God** in any way. But we speak of the mystery which is a mirror, wherein **God** and His perfections are seen in the works of this world in a quite special way.

But that the **multiplication of metals** <note>The alchemical process of increasing the quantity or potency of precious metals.</note> is not so common, nor is the seed so well-known to everyone as farming and viticulture; this is because few **Adepts** <note>original: *Adepti*. High-level alchemists who have successfully achieved the "Great Work."</note> instruct others in it and make them true artists <note>In alchemical literature, an "Artist" is a skilled practitioner of the chemical arts.</note>, and indeed, because of the great curse and for the sake of **God's** punishment. **God** is a searcher and tester of hearts and kidneys <note>A biblical idiom (similar to "hearts and minds") meaning that God examines a person's deepest motivations and character.</note>, and knows the evil lives of men, and that they would not do or begin much good with it. Therefore this high **secret** <note>original: *Secretum*</note> remains hidden, which the wise and pious alone possess.

<note>* Since we do not recognize this process as the one of which our wise masters testify in their writings with the most ingenious agreement, and which at another time, among a holy people on earth, shall become known to the faithful teachers and rulers; these words do not stop us from publishing it, all the less so as we do not see how its correctness—should it be discovered—could make many wicked people worse.</note>

# Chemical Experiments

In **this field** <note>In alchemy, the "field" or "soil" refers to the base material—the "prima materia"—in which the alchemist "plants" his seeds to grow gold.</note> we are spared the great effort required by the spirit and the salt of this process to separate so many impurities. In this, we already possess the **golden sulfur**, to which we know how to add the sulfur that lies within the body of gold to shorten the work. This twofold sulfur brings a **more-than-perfect gold** <note>original: *plusquamperfectes Gold*. A Latin-derived term for gold that has been refined beyond its natural state to a point where it can act as a "ferment" to transform other metals.</note> to light, which has the nature of a salt, once we have separated the earthly essence of the ferment from it using the **spirit of wine** <note>Ethanol or high-proof alcohol, used here as a solvent to extract the "essence."</note>. We therefore begin our work with the **gold ore** which heaven gives; not with a **strong water** <note>original: *starken Wasser*. Likely *Aqua Fortis* (nitric acid), commonly used by "false" alchemists to dissolve metals.</note>, and even less with a strong fire. Here is a single matter in which our **Saturn**, our **Antimony**, our **Green Lion**, our <term>Mercury</term> <note>☿</note>, <term>Antimony</term> <note>♁</note> and <term>Salt</term> <note>⊖</note>, our **doubled Mercury**, are hidden as our field—parts that emerge from it little by little through our fire.

### Whether this golden seed might also be good 
### for use as medicine?

That the **gold-seed**, from which gold has grown in a natural way, is the most precious medicine, is easy to consider. Since man consists of the **four elements** <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water; the classical building blocks of all matter.</note> and is, as it were, a pure extract of them, it follows that he has a special fellowship with the four elements and their fruits. No man can live without them. For who can do without air, water, earth, and fire? Since God created the elements for the preservation of human life, the elemental fruits must also serve man, primarily as food

# Chemical Experiments

* Health is far superior to wealth; for the sake of the latter, those seek a tincture who do not strive to fill their understanding with insights into the wonders of God, but rather seek to satisfy the animalistic desires of greed and ambition. It is easy to understand that one can expect the most excellent medicine from gold ore. However, just as God did not place the power to make one immortal into the fruits of that Tree of Life, so much less did He wish—after Adam’s transgression—to place a means for attaining immortality into nature. Instead, He intended to remain Lord over life and death through and without the means of nature. Therefore, no one should rely on an excellent medicine, but alone upon God as the unique Physician, who can grant His own people a healthy and long life through and without means, as the examples of the Fathers <note>Refers to the Biblical patriarchs, such as Methuselah, who lived for centuries.</note> before and after the Deluge <note>The Great Flood.</note> sufficiently prove. The Art <note>A common historical term for Alchemy.</note> has its validity, though exaggerated praise makes it suspect of falsehood.

# Chemical Experiments

Now follows the true description of how to bring gold to its growth, which is no deceit, but a holy truth.

The first and precious extraction of the **gold-seed** from the **gold-tree** happens thus:

Take eight pounds of pure and fine **gold ore** <note>original: *Minera*. In this context, it refers to raw, unrefined mineral ore containing gold.</note>, as it comes fresh from the mines, and which carries no other ore or quartz with it, other than pure gold alone. Pound or crush it into small pieces the size of a pea. Fill a strong glass and well-coated **retort** <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances.</note> half-full with it. For it must remain half empty. Wall it into a suitable furnace. **Seal it** <note>original: *Verlutire*. To seal a vessel with "lute," a leak-proof clay or cement used in high-heat alchemy.</note> very firmly. Attach a beautiful, large, clear, and strong <term>master flask</term> <note>original: *Herrnkolben*. A large glass receiving vessel used to collect the vapors that condense during distillation.</note> to it. When the <term>sealant</term> <note>original: *Lutum*. A paste used to join glass apparatus together to prevent the escape of vapors.</note> has dried well, gradually apply fire until the retort begins to grow warm. Afterwards, increase the fire, but very gradually from degree to degree. At such a heat, let it stand in <term>distillation</term> <note>The process of heating a substance to create vapor and then cooling the vapor to collect it as a liquid.</note> for 24 hours.

\* If we were to drive such a hare into the kitchen <note>A metaphorical expression meaning to provide someone with something substantial or "real results."</note> for kings and princes, they might at least judge that our philosophy is to be honored more than that which feeds them with general concepts, possibilities, and probable whims.

First, a bright clear water will come over: Second, a milk-colored fatty <term>spirit</term> <note>original: *Spiritus*. In early chemistry, this refers to a volatile liquid or essence produced by distillation.</note>, both of which [mix] with one another...

<meta>catchword: Auf</meta>

...to mix. This is the <term>radical moisture</term> <note>original: *humidum radicale*. In alchemical theory, this is the fundamental, life-giving fluid or "sap" believed to exist within all metals, necessary for their growth and transformation.</note> proper to the noble gold-tree.

> * When it happens this way, proceed.

After the vessels have cooled, pour the spirit very gently into a vial, which is to be well-sealed, placed in a cellar, and kept for use.

The retort in which the distillation took place is broken into pieces. The congealed gold is separated from the gray ash; it is then to be melted with borax and saltpeter and poured seven times through <term>antimony</term> <note>A brittle, silvery metal often used by alchemists to "wash" or purify gold, as it carries away impurities when melted together.</note>. In this manner, one obtains an uncommonly beautiful gold, which should be carefully kept for use.

> * Following the fashion, a wife and a harlot are assigned to this King <note>In alchemical allegory, "The King" represents Gold. The "wife" and "harlot" refer to other substances or chemicals added during the process that can either aid or hinder the gold's "fertility" or transformation.</note>. The latter prevents the fertility of the true wife, who is known only to the children of wisdom.

Grind the gray ash into a fine powder. Place it in a separation flask and pour a good distilled spirit of wine vinegar over it, so that the spirit stands a good hand’s breadth above the ash. Then the flask is to be well-sealed <note>original: *verlutirt*. Sealed with a paste or clay to make the vessel airtight.</note> and placed for eight days and nights in a gently warm <term>water bath</term> <note>original: *B. M.* (Balneum Mariae). A vessel of warm water used to heat another container slowly and evenly, preventing direct contact with the fire.</note> to <term>digest</term> <note>original: *digeriren*. The process of exposing a substance to steady, gentle heat to encourage chemical changes or extraction.</note>; thus the spirit turns completely milk-white. Pour this very gently into a vessel. Filter it. Afterwards, tie the vessel shut well and keep it in a warm place.

## Chemical Experiments

Upon the remaining ash which stayed in the <term>separation flask</term> <note>original: *Scheidkolben*. A glass vessel used for chemical separations, often similar to a retort.</note>, pour fresh spirit of wine vinegar again, just as the first time. Place it once more for 8 days and nights in a gently warm <term>water bath</term> <note>original: *B. M.* or *Balneum Mariae*. A vessel of hot water used to heat another container slowly and evenly, preventing overheating.</note> until the spirit has colored itself milky white again. Pour this off very slowly as well. Filter it and add it to the first portion that was set aside. This method and manner—the pouring on of fresh vinegar spirit and the <term>digestion</term> <note>original: *Digerirung*. The process of allowing a substance to sit in a solvent under gentle heat to extract its components.</note> in the water bath—is repeated three times. By then, all the power is extracted from the ashes. Pour all the milky-white extractions together into a clean balloon flask. Place an <term>alembic head</term> <note>original: *Helm* (literally "helmet"). The top part of a still that catches the rising vapors and directs them into a condenser.</note> upon it and distill it in the water bath until completely dry; a considerable amount of white crystalline salt will remain behind.

Upon this salt, pour a good portion of pure distilled rainwater, which must be made a little warm, and in which the salt of gold <term>dissolves</term> <note>original: *solviret*.</note>. This solution is filtered through paper and <term>coagulated</term> <note>original: *eincoaguliret*. Here, meaning to evaporate the liquid until the solid substance remains.</note> until completely dry in the water bath. One repeats this procedure <note>original: *Modum repetiret*.</note> three times.

The fourth time, dissolve this salt in <term>well-rectified Spanish spirit of wine</term> <note>original: *Spiritu vini hispanico bene rectificato*. High-proof alcohol distilled from Spanish wine, considered a superior solvent by early modern chemists.</note>. Digest the salt within it for 8 days and nights. Afterward, filter it through <term>blotting paper</term> <note>original: *Fließpapier*. An early form of absorbent paper used for filtration.</note>. <term>Distill off</term> <note>original: *abstrahiret*.</note> more than half in the water bath and place it in a cool, well-secured cellar; thus, a wonderful, very delicate salt will crystallize, which sparkles like a diamond.

# First Collection.

B
* This

This salt is taken out with a spoon and preserved very well in a glass vessel. One then distills half of the remaining part of the solution again in a <term>water bath</term> <note>original: *B. M.* or *Balneum Mariae*. A gentle heating method using hot water.</note>, and allows the salt to <term>crystallize</term> <note>original: *anschießen*. The process of salt crystals forming and precipitating out of a saturated solution.</note> just as before. This is repeated until the salt no longer wishes to crystallize.

This salt is entirely <term>wax-fluid</term> <note>original: *wachsflüßig*. A term describing a salt that melts easily like wax when heated, indicating high purity in alchemical practice.</note> and is now purified to the highest degree. It possesses a very pleasant scent and an exceedingly sweet taste. Seal this carefully so that its scent does not evaporate, and save it until it is to be used.

Next, one takes the <term>spirit</term> <note>original: *Spiritum*.</note> or the <term>radical moisture of gold</term> <note>original: *humidum radicale auri*. In alchemy, this refers to the fundamental, life-sustaining liquid essence believed to exist within the metal.</note> that was set aside earlier. Pour it from the vial into a small glass flask. Lute an <term>alembic head</term> <note>original: *Helm*. The cap of a distillation apparatus that directs vapors into a condenser.</note> onto it and attach a <term>receiver</term> <note>original: *Vorlag*. The vessel that collects the distilled liquid.</note>. Set this assembly into a <term>sand bath</term> <note>original: *Sandcapelle*. A container filled with sand and heated, providing a more intense and even heat than a water bath.</note> and apply a very gentle fire; in this way, everything rises clear and bright into the receiver. This distillation is continued until nothing remains behind. At that point, the radical moisture of gold is purified and <term>rectified</term> <note>original: *rectificiret*. The process of refining a liquid by repeated distillation to increase its potency or purity.</note> in the best possible way. It should weigh slightly more than one pound.

Of this <term>spirit of gold</term> <note>original: *Spiritu auri*.</note>, pour two <term>Loth</term> <note>A traditional German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 14 to 17 grams or about half an ounce.</note> onto nine Loth of the aforementioned <term>salt of gold</term> <note>original: *Salis auri*.</note>. Allow them to <term>coagulate</term> <note>original: *eincoaguliren*. To thicken or solidify into a unified mass through evaporation.</note> together very <term>gently</term> <note>original: *suaviter*.</note> on mildly warm sand; thus the body is reunited with its own spirit and prepared for a new birth.

Then this first work is completed.

## On the Multiplication of the Metallic
## Tincture <term>the "Tincture" is the alchemical substance capable of transforming base metals into gold or curing any illness</term>.

Take the large, beautiful ruby grain <term>likely a solidified, concentrated form of the red elixir or "Philosopher's Stone"</term>. Weigh it to see how heavy it is. Grind it very finely in a glass mortar. Afterward, add ten times its weight of the set-aside and impregnated gold salt <term>a chemical preparation of gold, often gold chloride, used as a "food" for the Tincture during the multiplication process</term>. Mix them well together. Then, put the mixture into a glass phial, which must be only half full. Seal it hermetically <note>original: *hermetice*; to seal a glass vessel by melting its neck shut so that no air or spirit can escape</note>. Then place it in a vapor bath <note>original: *balneum vaporis*; a method of indirect heating where the flask is suspended over steaming water</note> over the previously mentioned lamp light with a wick, and with very gentle heat; thus everything dissolves within 24 hours into a blood-red juice.

Afterward, leave it standing undisturbed for 40 days and nights; then the beautiful red color will gradually turn into a dark color, and finally again—as happened the first time—into an ink-colored blackness <note>the *Nigredo* or "blackening" stage, symbolizing the death and putrefaction of the substance before its rebirth</note>. After the 40 days have passed, take the phial out of the vapor bath and place it in gently warm ashes for 14 days; then this blackness transforms through all sorts of wonderful colors into the most beautiful grass-green, and within 14 days into a completely bright, shining silver-white. After this silver-white clarity has shown itself and stood for its allotted time; place the phial in warm sand and keep it there until the white color turns into a beautiful lemon-yellow, then to a lovely rose-red, and finally with...

### *Fermentation of the Tincture* <note>original Latin: *Fermentatio tincturæ*</note>

When the ruby <note>the concentrated red alchemical substance described in previous pages</note> has been <term>augmented</term> <note>the process of increasing the quantity and quality of the Stone</note> only 2 or 3 times, take one <term>loth</term> <note>a German unit of weight, approximately 14.5 grams or half an ounce</note> of it. Diligently save the rest as the most precious treasure. You will have at least six loth.

Grind that one loth somewhat small in a glass mortar. Then, take twelve loth of your saved and seven-times-refined superfine gold. Have a gold-beater hammer it as thin as gold leaf. Then, cut it into very small pieces. Divide it into two parts. Take one part and press it into a good, sturdy <term>crucible</term> <note>a ceramic container used for high-heat chemical reactions</note> that holds the fire well. Sprinkle the loth of gold-ruby upon it. After that, press the other part of the cut gold on top until all 12 loth are in the crucible. <term>Lute</term> <note>seal with a specialized airtight paste or clay</note> the top of the crucible very tightly with good <term>luting-clay</term> <note>original: *Luto*</note>. When it has dried well, place it for 12 hours in a <term>wind furnace</term> <note>a furnace designed to pull a strong draft of air to reach higher temperatures</note>, and give it fire <term>by degrees</term> <note>original Latin: *per gradus*; increasing the temperature slowly and systematically</note>; in this way, the single loth of the red essential ruby-stone will have transformed the 12 loth of fine gold into the most beautiful ruby, and into a pure, exceedingly powerful <term>tincture</term> <note>a potent substance capable of transmuting metals or healing illness</note>, which is an exceedingly great wonder of God. Break the crucible and take out the ruby, even scraping out the smallest crumbs as cleanly as possible. Save everything diligently, as it will perform wonders, specifically in the following manner:

### Projection <note>original Latin: *Projectio*; the final stage of the alchemical process where the "Stone" or Tincture is cast upon base metal to transmute it into gold</note>

Take one <term>grain</term> <note>a very small unit of weight, roughly 0.06 grams</note> of this aforementioned tincture. Wrap it in a little white or red wax. Form a pill from it, but in such a way that the tincture is placed right in the center; otherwise, the fire will cause it great harm. Next, take one pound of <term>quicksilver</term> <note>original Latin: *mercurium*; liquid mercury</note>. Place it in a crucible. Also add lead or tin. Let it become hot and melt. Or, create an <term>amalgam</term> <note>a mixture of metals, one of which is mercury, resulting in a softened or liquid state</note> with one pound of quicksilver and one pound of lead or tin, as you know how to do. Place one pound of this into a crucible. Let it melt. Afterward, throw in your pill and let it all melt together for a full hour. Then pour it out; you will find such a large piece of gold that you will be astonished that such a tiny grain demonstrated such an overflowing power. Now calculate what these 12 <term>loth</term> <note>a German unit of weight; 12 loth is approximately 6 ounces or 175 grams</note> of tincture are worth; you will find that they amount to several tons of gold. This is the true Universal Tincture from the **Gold-tree** <note>an alchemical metaphor for the philosophical process that "grows" precious metals</note> and its fruit.

> \* In gratitude for this communication <note>the sharing of this secret knowledge</note>, we ask for a small portion of it for ourselves, should anyone bring it so far. For our circumstances are not such that we can undertake such work ourselves. Moreover, we would not know how to go about it.

From this, everyone can see that gold is fruitful and not dead, but its natu-

\* It is certain that a wealthy person cannot be saved without becoming "rich" in these and similar works <note>acts of charity</note>. It is also certain, however, that among a thousand seekers of this art, hardly one works primarily for the service of the poor. They do not consider how God punished greed—which denies His providence—in the case of the **manna** in the wilderness <note>referring to the biblical story where those who hoarded more manna than they needed found it rotted</note>. Paul the Apostle sought an **equality** among the believers of his time regarding the necessary support for life. This equality has been abolished since ancient times by a "dead faith," for nothing testifies so clearly against a decay in doctrine and conduct as poverty and begging among Christians.

Since this is such a great treasure, God has hidden it so deeply in nature that only a few can reach it. For if every <term>laborant</term> <note>a practical chemist or laboratory worker</note> were to snatch such a high <term>arcanum</term> <note>original Latin: *arcanum*; a profound secret or mystery of nature</note>, what would become of him, and how much mischief would he cause with it? He would sell this treasure to the unworthy...

<summary>This page describes the "seed" of gold and emphasizes that the Tincture's true purpose is the charitable support of the poor, mirroring the communal ethics of the early Church. It warns that God hides these secrets to prevent the greedy and unworthy from causing social or spiritual ruin.</summary>

...[those] who would abuse such things to the great detriment of their temporal and eternal welfare.

* All things have their use and abuse. For a good use, we share what is good. Whoever abuses such things has the Giver of all good as a judge over him—a judge who has a thousand ways to put an end to the abuse. This <term>process</term> <note>the specific alchemical procedure for creating the Tincture or Philosopher's Stone</note> has qualities that will keep most people from attempting it. Even if it were entirely correct, it should still not succeed for those who harbor evil intentions. But for now, we still have some doubts about its accuracy. Therefore, let no one accuse us of being a breaker of the **seal of Hermes** <note>a traditional vow of secrecy among alchemists, named after the legendary founder Hermes Trismegistus</note>, whose ***emerald tablet*** <note>original: *smaragdene Tafel*; a foundational, cryptic text of alchemy said to contain the secret of the "one thing"</note> points toward the middle world <note>the terrestrial realm where humans live</note>, the secret of which shall remain the property of the wise of all times.

As for the pious, they will give God praise and thanks for this treasure, live with it in a quiet and holy manner, and do good to their neighbor. God will lengthen their lives and grant them a peaceful enjoyment of earthly goods—especially the faithful workers in the **Lord’s vineyard** <note>a biblical metaphor for those doing God's work on Earth</note>, who lie before the throne of God and sing: **Holy, holy, holy is the Lord of Hosts!** <note>original: *Zebaoth*; from the Hebrew *Sabaoth*, meaning the Lord of armies or celestial powers</note> To Him be praise, honor, glory, and thanks from now until eternity. Amen.

* God, who keeps His hand over the **secret of the wise**, tests hearts; and to whoever does not sacrifice their whole heart to Him, it will—if not remain hidden—bring a curse instead of a blessing.

->2.<-

Now we come to another type of <term>tincture</term> <note>a potent medicinal or transformative substance</note>, by which the seed of gold is extracted from the root of gold. This method is twenty times cheaper than the previous one, but in return, it requires ten times more work and greater diligence. Anyone can work out this process with the help of God, regardless of which country or place they may be in. However, it proceeds much more slowly and laboriously with regard to the <term>purification</term> <note>the physical and spiritual cleansing of a substance</note> of the salt; this is because Nature has already worked longer and more thoroughly on the gold-salt within the **golden tree** <note>an alchemical metaphor for the maturation of metals within the earth</note> and its fruit than it has on the coarse and unripe root. Yet, these two tinctures are equal to one another in their perfection; there is also no difference between them in power and virtues.

* This second process belongs with the first, and brings comfort to those who see no means to obtain the materials for the former. Even though it is far cheaper, it still costs more than many might imagine. Therefore, we advise no one to set their hand to it until the costs have been well considered. For although it has a high probability of success, the risk is great; even if success were a certainty, not one in a thousand <term>amateurs</term> <note>original: *Liebhaber*; those who love the "Art" of alchemy but may lack professional training</note> should undertake it if they have reason to be careful with their money. But if a wealthy man wishes to grant a profit to the suppliers and poor <term>laborants</term> <note>assistants or laboratory workers</note>, and to satisfy his own curiosity, then he may let his superfluous money circulate for the common good in this way as well.

<summary>This page introduces a secondary, less expensive alchemical process for creating a gold tincture, emphasizing that while it saves on material costs, it requires significantly more labor and financial risk.</summary>

### Description of the True Material for this Universal Work.

The ancient sages, who lived at different times and resided in various places, sought for their work a material that they could find in their own land. They did not only live in Oriental lands, where gold ore could be obtained for a small price, but for the most part, they also lived in Germany, where there is a lack of this ore. They therefore had to reach their goal by another path, which we wish to show here.

In many places in Europe, within the <term>bowels of the earth</term> <note>original Latin: *visceribus terræ*</note>, a mineral is found which appears silvery-bright to the eye and has not yet entered into any hard <term>coagulation</term> <note>the alchemical process where a liquid substance thickens or solidifies into a solid mass</note>, but has been left entirely raw and unripe by Nature, and is coated with the <term>sphere of Saturn</term> <note>original Latin: *sphæra Saturni*; in alchemical symbolism, Saturn represents lead</note>. This mineral is nothing other than a leaden <term>mass</term> <note>original Latin: *massa*</note>, and the true root of gold. For neither gold nor silver can be born unless Nature has first formed such a leaden mass in her workshop, into which the <term>star</term> or <term>seed of the Sun</term> <note>original Latin: *Astrum* or *semen Solis*; the "Sun" refers to the spiritual or physical essence of gold</note> was immediately and initially imprinted into the <term>Body of Saturn</term> <note>original Latin: *Corpus Saturni*; the physical substance of lead</note> through the influence of the heavens, as it was intended by Nature to become gold.

* Our <term>Saturn</term> <note>lead, or the specific mineral being discussed</note> is not to be sought beneath the earth, and we are spared the great effort and expense which are required for the purification of this Saturn. We add nothing to it,

But we indeed act upon it, not with a strong fire, and even less by means of a strong water <note>original: *starkes Wasser*; likely a reference to corrosive acids like Aqua Fortis</note>, but rather we bring forth our gleaming mineral <note>original Latin: *Mineram*</note> from a hidden place. Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and chemist</note> asks you as follows:

> Have you also seen the land where a man has led his wife, whose wedding was held in the House of Nature? Have you understood how everyone has seen this sulfur beside and with you? If you then desire that old women should practice your philosophy, then teach them this sulfur-whitening <note>original: *Schwefel-Weismachung*; a pun on the alchemical stage of "whitening" (albedo) and the German term for "making wise" or even "deceiving"</note>. Say to the common man: Come here and see: Now the water is divided, and the sulfur has gone out: it will return white, and dry up the waters.

Therefore, lead will very rarely be found in the mines in Germany that does not contain a considerable quantity <note>original Latin: *Copia*</note> of silver, and when such is separated in strong water <note>nitric acid</note>, a little gold.

* Saturn <note>the alchemical name for lead</note> is the spirit, silver is the body, and gold is the soul of our mercury. If you can separate <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note>, <term>Salt</term> <note>symbol: 🜔</note>, and <term>Sulfur</term> <note>symbol: 🜍</note> from our antimony <note>original: *Antimonio*</note> and unite them inseparably in one body, then you understand what Basil <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemical figure</note> in his book on the Universal, what the author of the *Great Peasant* <note>original: *grossen Bauern*; a contemporary alchemical treatise</note>, what Eugenius Philalethes <note>pseudonym for Thomas Vaughan</note>, what Batsdorf, Bernhard <note>Bernhard Trevisanus</note>, and such wise masters have testified.

This mineral <note>original Latin: *Minera*</note> is found in great abundance in Hungary, Wallachia, Transylvania, Styria, Carinthia, Carniola, Dalmatia, and in the Indies, and is called **glass ore** <note>original: *Glaserz*; historically referring to silver glance or argentite, a lead-grey mineral</note> by the inhabitants.

* No true possessor <note>The term "possessor" refers to an adept who has successfully achieved the Philosopher's Stone.</note> has ever openly named the matter which we first take into our hands; rather, it has been indicated by everyone in a way hidden from the unworthy. Since all things in the world originate from our **Mercury** <note>original Latin: *Mercurio*</note>, all names are applied to it—especially those of metals and minerals, through which the philosophers wished to signal the various properties and colors of their matter as it undergoes changes during the work. This man favored **Vitriol** <note>a sulfate mineral</note>, another **Mercury**, a third **Antimony**, but we favor **Water**. Search everywhere through the literal words, yet you will not find what comes to light from our Water through the Art. Still, search in the dew and rainwater; there you will find that more is hidden in the water than a common **chemist** <note>original: *Chymicus*</note> can believe.

The artists <note>a common term for alchemical practitioners</note> have called this mineral **Royal Saturnine, or Immature Electrum** <note>original Latin: *Saturninam regiam, seu Electrum immaturum*; Saturnine refers to lead-like qualities, while Electrum is a natural alloy of gold and silver</note>, because the gold is very frequently surrounded by the **star** <note>original: *astro*</note> or the **first entity of the Mercury of the Sun** <note>original Latin: *primo ente Mercurii Solis*; this refers to the spiritual or "astral" essence of gold</note>. Therefore, this **mineral** <note>original Latin: *Minera*</note> is named the root of the **Gold Tree**: for as long as it stands in its rock, it drives forth young sprouts and gives birth to gold.

* He who has evaluated a **volatile gold** <note>Gold in a non-fixed or gaseous state, often associated with the "spirit" of the metal</note> will easily believe that gold grows beneath the earth, and that the volatile is gradually boiled out into a **fixed** <note>Stable and solid; "boiling" refers to the natural heat of the earth maturing the metal</note> state. Whatever grows must have a seed. But do you understand from where this **seed of gold** comes into the earth? We say: from above. You do not believe it, and as a scorner of the Art, no artist will seek to convince you.

> <margin>\* Regarding the Great Work, one does not need to beg anyone to provide the material for money and kind words. We only need to bend down to pick it up. ## True and Detailed Description of the Process of This Second Matter for This Universal.

Take several pounds of this pure mineral. Grind it as fine as flour, and wash it with clean, fresh water for as long as it takes until all the quartz and filth are removed, and the water runs off as clear as it was when poured on. Afterward, dry the **ore concentrate** <term>Schlich: a technical mining term for finely crushed and washed ore</term> thoroughly. When it is completely dry, take one pound after another and place them into various strong earthen **flasks** <note>original: *Kolben*; likely long-necked boiling flasks</note> which are wide at the bottom. Then pour a good, common **Spirit of Salt with its watery part** <note>original Latin: *Spiritum salis cum suo phlegmate*; this is hydrochloric acid in a diluted form</note> over it, so that the Spirit stands two good hand-widths above it. When this is done, cover the flasks with **blind glass helms** <note>original: *blinden Glashelmen*; an alembic head without a spout or delivery tube, used for refluxing rather than distilling</note>.

# Chemical Experiments

->First Collection.<-

* Although these tinctures might be entirely correct, they would nonetheless be so difficult to make that only a few could flatter themselves with the hope of bringing such a work to a desired end. To anyone who wishes to hold this *publication* against us, let them consider that a time has appeared wherein the secrets of God are not to be covered up, but rather to be revealed. In this, we harbor nothing but intentions pleasing to God, and we dedicate this good work to the faithful stewards over the temporal gifts of God, regardless of their station in life. This faithfulness is to be proven through good works. Among all people, kings, princes, and lords are the most capable of this. "Yes," you say, "these [great men] will abuse such a treasure in this or that way." Since you have been a possessor [of this knowledge] until now, tell me: have you performed good works in proportion to the value of such a treasure? You say: "I did not want to reveal myself for such and such reasons." Behold, you cannot and will not serve everyone with it. And why does it annoy you if God wishes to remedy the poverty among mankind through many officials on Earth, from whom He will demand an accounting? Examine your own heart to see whether greed, ambition, envy, and lust have not awakened such great displeasure within you against this undertaking. For we fear abuse less from the side of the Great <note>Refers to the nobility and ruling class.</note> than from the side of those who lock their treasure away in a chest and do not allow it to be shared among others.

### Concerning the *Multiplication* <term>multiplicatio</term> <note>original: "Multiplication". Multiplication is the alchemical process of increasing the quantity or the power of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> of these both *Tinctures*.

->First Collection.<-

The great ruby-grain can be multiplied by means of the *Salt of Saturn* <note>original: "Salis Saturni"; historically this refers to lead acetate, used here as a medium to increase the quantity of the alchemical substance.</note> as often as desired.

One continues with the metallic tincture and augments it likewise. Take the red grain with ten times its weight in salt, and proceed as has been described in detail above; in this way, one will obtain a large quantity of a very powerful tincture. Furthermore, the augmentation becomes shorter with each new repetition. It is unnecessary to write everything twice, as it is done the same way every time.

### *Concerning Fermentation.*

Take half a *quintlein* <note>A small historical unit of weight, roughly equal to 3.6 to 4 grams.</note> of the tincture once it has been multiplied 5 or 6 times. In a glass mortar, mix this into one thousand half-*quintleins* of small and fine *gold-calx* <note>original: "Goldkalk"; gold that has been chemically reduced to a fine, pure powder through calcination.</note> made in the common manner and way. Put these two items into a double and sturdy crucible. *Lute* it <note>original: "Verlutire"; to seal the lid of the crucible with a fire-resistant clay paste known as "lute" to make it airtight.</note> very well. When the *lute* <note>original: "Lutum"</note> has become dry, place the crucible in a gently covered *draft furnace* <note>original: "Windofen"; a furnace designed to draw in air to achieve high, consistent temperatures.</note> for three hours, and certainly no longer. Let it gradually heat to a glow, but indeed not too long, so that it does not melt: otherwise, the tincture would pass through the crucible like an oil <note>Alchemists believed that if the heat was too high or the substance too "subtle," it would leak through the pores of the ceramic vessel.</note>. Afterwards, let the crucible cool, and you will find a thousand parts of the best and most magnificent fermented tincture. Take one grain of it, and

### How to make Mercury from Cinnabar?

Take three pounds of common cinnabar <note>original: "Zinnober"; a bright red mineral, mercury(II) sulfide, which was the primary source of mercury for alchemists</note>. Crush and grind it very finely in a glass mortar, or on a grinding stone, so that it becomes like the finest painter's pigment. Mix into it two pounds of the finest and purest iron filings. Afterward, put this into an earthen, strong, well-coated retort <note>original: "Retorte"; a glass or ceramic bulb with a long neck used for distilling substances</note>. Fill it almost completely, so that no more than two fingers' breadth remains empty. Place the retort into the furnace upon a support. Secure it well with clay <note>original: "Leimen"; a binding paste</note> so that it remains lying firmly. 

After that, arrange a large receiver <note>original: "Recipienten"; the vessel designed to collect the products of distillation</note>, which is half full of water, at the retort. Lute it well <note>original: "Verlutire"; to seal the connection between vessels using a fire-resistant paste</note>. When the lute <note>original: "Lutum"</note> is quite dry, apply a very gentle fire; then a very beautiful "living" mercury passes over in the form of a white vapor, and all the cinnabar is refined <note>original: "rectificiret"; a process of purification through heat</note> such that little of it remains behind. When the distillation is finished, detach the receiver. Pour off the water very slowly, so that no mercury goes with it. Then pour

Then one can press it a few more times through a clean sheepskin <note>Sheepskin or chamois was used as a fine filter; the liquid mercury passes through the pores while solid impurities are left behind</note>; thus it is ready. This mercury is very noble and almost equal to gold.

Now follows how this mercury must ascend seven times into the heavens and descend again to the earth, whereby it is purified <note>original: "purificiret"</note> to the highest degree and also mortified <term>mortificiret|In alchemy, 'mortification' refers to the symbolic death of a substance, stripping it of its outward form or properties—in this case, its fluidity—to prepare it for transformation</term>, so that it can no longer flow at all but is entirely powerless.

Take half a pound of this mercury, and no more. Put it into a glass flask <note>original: "Kolbenglas"</note>. Place a helm <term>Helm|The 'alembic' or cap of a distilling apparatus that captures rising vapors and directs them into a cooling beak</term> upon it. Seal it <note>original: "Verslutire"; to seal joints with a fire-resistant clay paste to prevent vapors from escaping</note> well. Also attach a large bottle-glass [receiver] in front of it. Arrange the flask in a sand bath <term>Sandcapelle|A vessel filled with sand used to distribute heat evenly and safely around a glass flask over a fire</term> and apply a gentle fire, as you must understand; thus the mercury rises into the helm, as into the heavens, and passes through the beak into the receiver <note>original: "Vorlage"</note>, which is compared to the earth. 

This distillation is carried out until all the mercury has passed over. In this way, the mercury is highly refined <note>original: "rectificiret"</note> and subtilized <note>original: "subtilisiret"; the process of making a substance more refined, potent, or spiritually 'thin'</note>, and is so excellently boiled out by the fire through a sevenfold repetition that not the slightest impure speck remains with it. This method <note>original: "Modum"</note> is thus repeated seven times in succession. However, one must always rinse the flask clean, and the mer-

### Chymical Experiments

Press it firmly together. After that, place a good quantity of the earth upon the straw which has been placed into the barrel. Pour pure and clean water onto the earth, as much as the quantity of the earth may require. Let it stand for 24 hours so that the water may draw the pure salt from the earth into itself. Then, place a clean wooden barrel under the tap-hole and let the poured-on water run out very slowly through the tap-hole at the bottom, just as one makes lye <note>A liquid solution made by leaching minerals (usually from wood ash or earth) through water; here it refers to the mineral-rich water extracted from the "Virgin Earth"</note>.

A large quantity of lye must be made from this earth. When one has gathered a good supply of this lye, one fills a bricked-in copper kettle with it and lets it boil down by more than half. Afterwards, one fills the kettle up again with more lye and lets it boil down that far once more. This filling and boiling down is repeated 8, 9, or up to ten times.

When it has been boiled down for the last time and the kettle is only half full, take another copper kettle, bury it in fresh earth <note>Burying the vessel in the ground provided insulation for a very slow, stable cooling process, which was essential for the growth of high-quality crystals</note>, and pour the boiled lye into it. Let the lye stand for several days, completely undisturbed; thus a salt grows in the kettle like saltpeter <note>Potassium nitrate, a key ingredient in gunpowder, known for its needle-like crystals</note>, but it does not burn as saltpeter does. 

This salt must then be processed by <term>dissolving|Solviren: To dissolve the solid salt back into a liquid state</term>, <term>filtering|Filtriren: Straining the liquid through a medium to remove physical impurities</term>, <term>crystallizing|Anschiessen lassen: To allow the purified salt to form into crystals from a saturated solution</term>, and <term>coagulating|Coaguliren: Thickening or solidifying the substance</term> through de- <meta>catchword: distilled</meta>

* Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist who proposed that a "life-food" or "niter" existed in the air</note> and other wise masters along with him have found their **Niter** <note>original: *Nitrum*; while it can mean saltpeter, in this context it refers to a mystical mineral salt believed to be the vehicle for the world-spirit</note> by a better and easier way. From where has this **Niter** of the earth come? That which has been begotten can, in its own time, beget again, once it has been brought to its maturity through the separation of the pure from the impure. No artist <note>In the early modern period, "artist" specifically referred to a practitioner of alchemy or chemistry</note> knew everything alone, and one always knew something that remained unknown to another. We are therefore not like many who were overhasty in their knowledge and rejected everything that did not immediately seem to align with their own methods. Many small streams come from a single source, which through their course have changed their original water by taking on foreign matters. If you can separate these foreign matters, you will arrive at our water. However, we believe you would be spared a great deal of effort if you were to follow the small streams in order to find our source.

This <term>Salt of nature|Sal naturæ</term>, although it appears to be only a single thing to the eye, yet contains three distinct salts within it. For 1) it is in itself the philosophical <term>Niter from the earth|Nitrum ex terra</term>, in which the <term>Spirit of the world|Spiritus mundi</term> dwells abundantly. It is neither too volatile nor too fixed, but rather possesses a middle nature. 2) It contains a <term>Sal ammoniac|Sal ammoniacum</term> or <term>volatile salt|Sal volatile</term>. 3) There also resides in it an <term>Alkali|Alcali</term> or <term>fixed salt|Sal fixum</term>. It is therefore triune <note>The author draws a parallel between the chemical composition of the salt and the Holy Trinity, a common theme in "Spagyric" or religious alchemy</note>. All of this is revealed through the preparation by fire, as follows.

->*Preparation of the spirit of the salt of nature from our salt.*<-

Take one pound of the beautiful white salt of the earth prepared above. Crush it finely. Mix with it 3 pounds of that earth from which the salt was previously extracted. <note>The "dead" earth left over from the first extraction is reused here as a base to prevent the salt from melting too quickly during distillation</note> Note: the earth must first be thoroughly calcined <note>original: *ausgeglühet*; heated to a high temperature until it glows</note> in a potter's kiln, but in such a way that it does not melt. When the earth has become cold again, sprinkle it with pure water. Afterwards, make small round <term>pellets|Kügelein</term> from it, about the size of those that children play with. Let them become dry. ### Now follows the reunion of the three principles, namely the purified fixed salt, volatile salt, and spirit.

Hitherto you have separated **body, soul, and spirit** <note>The alchemical triad: the fixed salt (body), the volatile salt (soul), and the liquid essence (spirit)</note> from one another, and purified each one individually, as much as possible, from its **foreign matter** <note>original: *heterogeneis*; dissimilar or impure components</note> through the work of the fire. * *In the Great Work <note>The "Magnum Opus" or the creation of the Philosopher's Stone</note>, the union of the principles has a very different character. There, one also separates and purifies, and our gold—when united with a chaste bride who has been composed of spirit and body—is the third part.*

This Spirit is the true **universal solvent** <note>original: *menstruum universale*; a substance believed to be capable of dissolving all matter into its primary components</note>, with which all metals gladly unite and, as it were, **rejuvenate themselves**. For it is the **first matter** <note>original: *prima materia*; the original, formless substance from which all elements were believed to originate</note>, from which all metals were initially conceived and born in the **bowels of the earth** <note>original: *visceribus terræ*</note>. Following this description of the **Spirit of the Salt of Nature**, nothing is missing from the entire process. I therefore conclude and say: **To God be praise and glory forever and ever. Amen.** <note>original: *Deo sit laus & gloria in sæculorum sæcula. Amen.*</note>

Certainly, without this art you are neither a philosopher nor a student of nature <note>original: *Naturkündiger*; one who interprets or knows the secrets of the natural world</note>. Without it, you are merely a pharmacist <note>original: *Apotheker*</note> working for the doctors, or a laboratory assistant <note>original: *Laborant*</note> working for the wholesalers <note>original: *Materialisten*; in this era, the term referred to merchants who traded in raw materials, spices, and chemical bulk goods rather than those following a philosophical path</note>. Without such art, you do not understand the origin of things; nor do you know minerals, nor plants, nor animals—least of all yourself. For you consider yourself to be what Pythagoras and Plato taught, and what their disciples believed—men who so exceeded themselves in their thoughts that they are understood by others as little as they understand themselves. See, the world is full of incomprehensible articles of faith. We therefore point back once more to the two pure sources: Scripture and Nature. There, let your reason draw from the Word and the Spirit; thus you will learn to understand that what is called foolishness among men is wisdom with God. This wisdom is not to be found among many of the learned, because most prefer [physical] bread to wisdom and do not wish to take a single step against the rules of their guilds <note>original: *Zünften*; the rigid professional organizations that controlled trades and education</note>.

### 3.
## A Tincture for Silver and Mercury into Gold.

Take <note>original: *℞.* or *recipe*; the traditional symbol for a medical or alchemical prescription</note> common mercury <note>original: *Mercurium*; quicksilver</note>, as much as you wish, and purify it thus: Take vitriol <note>iron or copper sulfate</note>, alum, calcined <note>burnt to a powder</note> tartar <note>original: *Weinstein*; potassium bitartrate</note>, well-ground common salt, and quicklime <note>original: *lebendigen Kalk*; calcium oxide</note>: a handful of each. Put everything into an unglazed pot <note>original: *Hafen*</note>. Pour sharp wine vinegar onto it. Place the mercury inside and cover it well. One-third of the pot should remain empty; however, the lid on top should have a small opening.

When it seems to you that the moisture is gone, remove the crucible from the coals. Let it cool, and keep the amalgam—which must be protected from dust and moisture—for future use.
