### Augmentation. <term>Augmentatio; the stage of alchemy where the quantity or potency of the alchemical medicine is increased</term>

When you have 50 **lots** <term>Loth; a historical unit of weight, roughly 14.6 to 17 grams</term> of the **mercury** <term>Mercurius</term> that has been changed into gold, take one lot of the aforementioned **tincture** <term>Tinctur; a concentrated substance capable of transmuting metals</term>. Melt it together with this gold. Place them together in a glass that can be well sealed. Let it stand for eight weeks; in this way, they will become a tincture together. Take one lot of it and apply it to 50 lots of **purified mercury** <note>original: "Mercurii purgati"</note>; they will then become good gold.

It is necessary that whoever wishes to make this or a similar tincture must, above all things, be well-experienced in **alchemy**, have paid his dues <note>original: "sein Lehrgeld gegeben"; literally "paid his tuition money," an idiom meaning to have learned through costly experience and trial and error</note>, and especially have wrestled, struggled <note>original: "vexirt"; from the Latin 'vexare', used in alchemy to describe the process of agitating or "torturing" a substance to release its essence</note>, and thoroughly tested himself with **mercury**. He must know the nature of the furnaces and the **management of the fire** <note>original: "Regiment des Feuers"; the precise control of heat intensity required for different chemical stages</note>, as well as the **true philosophical amalgamation and solution** <note>original: "amalgamationem & solutionem philosophicam veram"</note> regarding the **first matter** <note>original: "quoad primam materiam"; the primordial substance from which all things originate and the starting point for the Philosopher's Stone</note>, likewise **sublimation** <term>sublimationem; the process of heating a substance into vapor so it condenses back into solid form in a purer state</term> and **fixation** <term>fixationem; the process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to heat</term> with their glass vessels and **alembics** <term>Alembiken; distillation apparatus consisting of a boiling vessel and a cooling cap with a delivery tube</term> in which to work. Otherwise, no one can make such a tincture: know this for a truth.

*Among the many processes involving **mercury** that we have in hand, we give preference to this one. For although this **volatile servant** <note>original: "flüchtige Knecht"; a common alchemical nickname for mercury due to its liquid, elusive nature and how easily it vaporizes</note> does not otherwise let himself be easily bound, such subtle techniques are used here that we believe anyone who possesses the qualities required in the conclusion, along with patience, may safely undertake it without the risk of bringing significant harm to himself or others through failed efforts.*

### 4.

## A Tincture which, by virtue of its usefulness, is rightly called a *Philosopher's Stone* <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*</note>.

All metals can be augmented <term>Augmentation; the process of increasing the quantity or power of a substance</term> through art and diligence by means of **mercury** <term>Mercurium</term>, and can be brought back into their first, proper, and sufficiently mature substances or seeds. All metals carry their own **mercury** within them, which can be visibly extracted <note>original: "ausgewogen"; literally "weighed out," here meaning to separate and quantify the metallic essence</note> with skill. In particular, many great secrets are contained within the **mercuries of gold, silver, and lead** <note>original: "Mercuriis Solis, Lunæ & Saturni"</note>. For if you were to extract their **mercury** from these metals and work through the process as follows hereafter, the benefit drawn from it would be no less than that which is expected from the **Philosopher’s Stone**. The following tincture, which is prepared with the **mercury of gold** <note>original: "Mercurio Solis"</note>, possesses no less power and virtue than the philosophical stone, which the ancients sought and crafted with long-enduring toil and labor. ### Water for the Dissolution of Gold.

Take <note>original: "℞," the recipe symbol for *recipe*</note> good and well-purified saltpeter and calcined <term>calcination; the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to remove volatile components or reduce it to powder</term> vitriol, in equal parts <note>original: "ana"</note>. Put this water into a glass flask <term>Kolbenglas; a round-bottomed flask used in distillation</term>, and for every pound of liquid, add 6 *Loth* <note>A *Loth* is a historical German unit of weight, roughly 15 to 17 grams</note> of sal ammoniac <term>Salmiac; ammonium chloride, used here to convert the nitric acid into aqua regia</term>. Let the solution stand for a day and a night. Place it in moist, cold sand in a cellar; beautiful yellow-red crystals will then form <note>original: "schiessen"; literally "shoot"</note>. Carefully pour off the water from them and preserve the crystals. Afterward, further draw off the moisture from the water that was poured off the crystals, down to the spirits, but not too harshly. Place it back in the cellar in cold sand; more crystals will form. Repeat this until all the gold has turned into crystals and nothing remains in the water. You may also pour a small drop of rectified spirit of wine <note>original: *spiritu vini rectificato*; concentrated alcohol</note> into it; this will cause the crystals to form all the sooner. When this is accomplished, pour the following oils three finger-widths high over the crystals in a vial, which is then to be placed in horse manure for putrefaction <note>original: *fimum equinum ad putrefactionem*; decaying manure was used by alchemists as a natural, steady heat source for slow "digestion" or fermentation</note>, and not in a water bath <note>original: *B. ### Excerpt from the Epistle of *Theophrastus* to *D. Barthol. Korndörfer* concerning the *Clarification* and *Precipitation* of Metals.

Our *alchemists* insist on making gold and silver only from unclarified bodies, and as a result, they find nothing but dross. I send to you herein the true foundation of the <term>Mercury; the essential liquid principle of metals</term> of all metals: how to <term>precipitate; to cause a substance to settle into a solid form</term> and <term>tinge; to alchemically transform or color a metal</term> them from one into another by a short path. When you possess the true foundation for dissolving all <term>bodies; the solid, physical form of metals</term>, for <term>clarifying; the process of purifying a substance of its impurities</term> them, and for bringing them into their running and dry water, then you will achieve more with them than I have explained to you in my *Practica* <note>Paracelsus’s "Practica" usually refers to his specific laboratory instructions or applied medicinal teachings</note>. Although several vagabonds force certain metals into their liquid essence by constraint—specifically with many absurd *corrosive species* which are more harmful to the metals, causing them to lose their best power—this is not the path that belongs to my *precipitation* of metals. But you, as a faithful researcher of this art, will undoubtedly also encounter my short path *concerning the resuscitation of metals* <note>original Latin: *de resuscitatione metallorum*; the process of restoring a metal to its "living" or active state</note> in the meantime. *Philippus Theophrastus* <note>A common signature of Paracelsus</note> [Year] 37.

The first metal, *Saturn* <note>original: *Saturnus*; the alchemical name for lead</note>, *precipitates* itself with its *clarified* body alongside its own kind, until it strikes itself into a *precipitate*. ## Explanation and Basis of the Aforementioned Philosophy

Take 3 <term>Loth; a historical unit of weight, approximately 14.5 grams</term> of the **mercury of Saturn** <note>original: *Mercurii Saturni*; the "mercurial" or liquid essence extracted from lead</note> and one Loth of the **mercury of the Sun** <note>original: *Mercurii Solis*; the liquid essence extracted from gold</note>. To this, add a <term>quarter-weight; an "Ort," representing a quarter of a coin's weight</term> <note>original: *Ort*</note> of a Gulden of pure **gold calx** <note>original: *Goldkalk*; gold reduced to a fine powder through heating or chemical action</note> to serve as a **ferment** <note>original: *Ferment*</note> or seed. For whatever seed you sow in this field, that you shall harvest. Precipitate this according to custom in a <term>vial; a glass laboratory flask</term> <note>original: *Phiole*</note>, until it has become a fixed precipitate.

To this fixed precipitate, add again as much of the mercury of Saturn as the precipitate weighs. However, always add a little gold calx first, and precipitate it again into a fixed precipitate. This you may **augment** <note>original: *augmentiren*; to increase the quantity or potency</note> as often as you wish. However, it transforms no more than 6 parts of silver into gold.

But when this precipitate is again soaked with the mercury of the Sun and precipitated; then it transforms 30 parts of silver into gold.

This process also deserves an investigation into its truth. Whoever is able and willing to venture it, and possesses the required skill; we shall not prevent them from setting their hand to the task. When one **sublimes** <note>the process of heating a solid until it becomes a vapor and then cooling it back into a solid to purify it</note> **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> with **Vitriol** <note>a sulfate mineral; alchemists believed it contained a hidden "fire"</note>, which, as the **Sulfur of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Sulphur Philosophorum*; not common sulfur, but a theoretical principle of fixity and color</note>, has an invisible nature of redness hidden within it; then the two **Mercuries** come together, and the weight of the **sublimate** increases in every **sublimation**. In every **sublimation**, the combustibility <note>the tendency to be consumed by fire</note> is taken from the **Mercury**, and the spirit of **Roman Vitriol** <note>original: *Römischen Vitriols*; often referring to high-quality iron or copper sulfate</note> is infused, which contains an abundant redness within itself. We make our **Stone** <note>the Philosopher's Stone</note> from **Mercury** and **Sulfur**; from a **Mercury** derived from ore or another proven source, which we **sublime** with **Roman Vitriol** <note>original: *Vitriolo Romano*</note> and **saltpeter** <note>potassium nitrate, used as a powerful oxidizing agent</note>. When the **sublimation** has reached a proper whiteness, one finds that for every pound of **Mercury**, there are 4 **Loth** <note>a historical weight unit; 4 Loth is approximately 2 ounces or 60 grams</note> additional; this increase in weight comes from the spirit of the **Vitriol**.

### *Calcination*
<note>original: *Calcinatio*; the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or "lime"</note>

Take <note>original: ℞, the symbol for *recipe*</note> the aforementioned <term>quintessence</term> <note>original: *Quintessenz*; the "fifth essence" or purest, most concentrated part of a substance</note>. Grind it as finely as possible on a stone. Put it into a round glass or <term>philosophical egg</term> <note>original: *ovum philosophicum*; a round-bottomed flask with a long neck, used for slow heating and digestion</note>, which is to be well-sealed. Place this in the <term>secret furnace</term> <note>original: *Ofen der Heimlichkeit*; a specialized furnace designed for maintaining precise, constant, and often low temperatures</note>, which I will describe below. Give it a gentle fire for 6 to 8 days, so that it may be digested, cooked, and prepared for dissolution. For without this cooking, it will not dissolve. This operation is a key to the Art, and is called a *Calcination* by some masters.

### *Composition of Virgin's Milk*
<note>original: *Compositio lactis virginis*; an alchemical term for a white, milky solution or precipitate often used as a stage in the transformation of matter</note>

->*The Separation of the Three Elements*<-
<note>original: *Separatio trium elementorum*</note>

Take <note>original: ℞, the symbol for *recipe*</note> the aforementioned burnt *dregs* <note>original: *Feces*; the solid remains or sediment left over after distillation</note>. Place them into the chamber of the *athanor* <note>original: *Athanors*; a furnace designed to provide a steady, uniform heat over a long period</note>, and calcine them as was shown above regarding the <term>quintessence</term>. After the calcination, place them in the bath for *putrefaction* <note>original: *Putrefaction*; a stage of digestion where the substance is allowed to decay or break down through gentle warmth</note>. Then distill them through a strong fire of the bath, so that the air rises up as an *oil* <note>original: *oleum*</note>. If it does not wish to rise easily, then force it with the ashes <note>original: *Aschen*; referring to an ash-bath, which allowed for higher temperatures than a water-bath</note>. Distill in this manner until nothing more rises. Thereupon, place the glass in a fire of *sublimation* <note>original: *Sublimation*; heating a substance until it becomes a vapor and then collects again as a solid</note>, and intensify it until the fire rises as a red oil and nothing more passes over. Keep each of these two elements separately for use. Now you have separated the 4 elements of our *mercury* <note>original: *Mercurii*</note>. For the earth remains at the bottom as a coal which is of no use. If you have understood all of this, then it is well. But if not, then let another explain it to you.

...of the bath <note>original: *canorum*; likely referring to a *balneum vaporis* or similar steam bath</note>, or in warm horse manure, the heat of which one can increase with <term>quicklime</term> <note>original: *ungelöschten Kalk*; calcium oxide, which releases significant heat when it reacts with moisture</note>. Give it such a gentle fire that you can endure your hand in it at all times. Drive this day and night continuously until you see that the matter begins to turn black and enters into <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: *Fäule*; a crucial alchemical stage where the substance is 'broken down' by gentle warmth</note>. When the blackness lingers too long, you may increase the fire slightly. When you see the blackness, rejoice, because it is the true beginning of a good hope. Keep the fire neither larger nor smaller until you see many different colors in the glass. When the matter begins to turn white, then increase your fire gradually, so that it can hardly be felt. Continue in this manner until perfect whiteness is achieved.

For the white <term>tincture</term> <note>original: *Tinctur*; a concentrated medicinal or alchemical essence</note>, this way is the best. Others mix three elements together for this: one part fire, four parts air, and eight parts water. However, this way is the best for the Red, and the worst for the White.

### Preparation of the Red Stone
<note>original: *Preparatio lapidis rubei*</note>

<term>Take</term> <note>original: ℞, the standard symbol for *recipe*</note> 1 part fire, 4 parts air, and 8 parts water. Put these three elements together in a glass, and rule the fire just as was stated above for the white tincture, until the perfect whiteness has appeared. Then the stone fears the increasing fire no more, which is to be strengthened degree by degree until the stone has attained its perfect redness.

If one wishes to continue boiling the white <term>tincture</term> <note>original: *Tinctur*; a concentrated alchemical essence or "medicine" capable of transforming matter</note> by increasing the intensity of the fire, it will likewise turn red without the addition of Fire and Air, which are contained more subtly in <term>virgin’s milk</term> <note>original: *lacte virginis*; a term for a specific white solvent or distilled substance used in the work</note>. ->*On Multiplication*<-
<note>original: *De Multiplicatione*</note>

One can increase this Medicine for White and Red in both quantity and quality as often as one wishes, by dissolving it in a <term>bath</term> <note>original: *Balneo*; likely a *balneum Mariae* or water bath</note>, adding the elements, and <term>coagulating</term> <note>original: *Coaguliren*; the process of turning a liquid into a solid</note> it, as was taught above.

->*On Projection*<-
<note>original: *De Projectione*; the final stage where the alchemical medicine is "cast" upon molten base metals to transform them into silver or gold</note>

This Medicine is powerful enough to heal the diseases of the imperfect metals and to bring them to that perfection for which the Creator intended them. Nature desires a perfection which she could not achieve because of an accidental and unclean soil, or through the influence of adverse planets and stars. The Medicine removes these impurities by casting out a small part of them, and the metals are changed by it, just as a piece of <term>leaven</term> <note>original: *Hefel*; yeast or starter dough used to ferment and raise bread</note> transforms a large mass of flour into bread.

As proof of this, take as much <term>mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurio*; quicksilver</note> as you wish. Let it warm in a crucible until it smokes. Then add a bit of the Medicine—whether it be the white or the red—and give it a strong fire, just as if you were intending to melt silver...

But whatever has settled at the bottom, pour <term>philosophical vinegar</term> <note>original: *acetum philosophicum*; a potent alchemical solvent, often a highly rectified vinegar or a specialized mineral acid</note> over it again, and dissolve the powder in it. Draw off the vinegar again. Pour fresh <term>dew-water</term> <note>original: *Thauwasser*; water collected from morning dew, believed by alchemists to contain a celestial or "astral" energy</note> onto it. Place it in the ashes <note>referring to an ash bath, a common method of providing gentle, steady, and indirect heat</note>, as before, for 24 hours; in this way, more of the powder will dissolve. Proceed thus until you have completely dissolved the powder in the dew-water. Afterwards, draw off the dew-water <term>by means of an alembic</term> <note>original: *per alembicum*; a distilling apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube or "beak"</note>; there will remain a subtle and almost <term>impalpable</term> <note>meaning a powder so fine it cannot be felt between the fingers</note> powder.

℞. <note>The "Recipe" or "Prescription" symbol.</note> Take this powder and divide it into three parts. Place one part of it into a vial, which is to be sealed with wax and buried in a damp cellar for ten days; thus it dissolves into a yellow water, which is the <term>potable gold</term> <note>original: *aurum potabile* or *Trinkgold*; a legendary liquid gold elixir intended for medicinal consumption</note> and the soul.

Place the second part on your hand. Add a sixteenth part of <term>salt of tartar</term> <note>original: *Weinsteinsalz*; potassium carbonate, typically obtained by burning the crust formed in wine barrels</note> to it. Rub both together in your hand with a finger; thus it becomes a running <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurius*; here it refers to the liquid metallic state, the "spirit" of the substance</note>. Keep this, and drive it <term>through the alembic</term> <note>distill it</note> just like the <term>potable gold</term>; thus the <term>tartar remains at the bottom</term> <note>original: *Tartarus in fundo*</note>, which is to be discarded as useless. This purified Mercury is the spirit of our stone <note>referring to the Great Work or the Philosopher's Stone</note>.

> ->Note: the method for the distillation of the potable gold by means of a water bath and the sublimation of our Mercury is one and the same.<- <note>original: *Nota, destillationis per balneum auri potabilis & sublimationis nostri Mercurii unum esse modum.*</note>

Place the third part of the aforementioned powder back into the dew-water. Keep it therein until you wish to combine the parts.

<summary>This page details the extraction of the "soul" (potable gold) and "spirit" (mercury) from a refined gold powder using dew-water and salt of tartar. These components are then set aside to be reunited in the final stages of the alchemical process.</summary>

...you wish. For if you were to let the lime <note>original: *Kalk*; in alchemy, a "calx" or fine powder produced by heating a metal until it loses its metallic form</note> lie thus dry in the air, it would dissolve itself into <term>potable gold</term> <note>original: *aurum potabile*; a legendary liquid gold elixir intended for medicinal consumption</note>; so that it would not be a body, but a soul <note>original: *anima*; the "soul" of a metal was thought to be its essential color and medicinal virtue</note>.

Thus you have dissolved our Stone, and divided it into three parts, namely, into Soul, Spirit, and Body. From the Spirit and the Soul you have removed the superfluous things, and purified them <term>by sublimation</term> <note>original: *per sublimationem*; the process of heating a substance until it becomes vapor and then solidifies again, leaving impurities behind</note>, but the Body you have purified <term>by reverberation and dissolution</term> <note>original: *per reverberationem & solutionem*; "reverberation" involves heating in a furnace where flames are reflected back onto the material; "dissolution" is the act of dissolving it into a liquid state</note> according to the sayings of the Philosophers.

Just as you have extracted and divided the body, the soul, and the spirit from the gold body: do the same also with the <term>lunar body</term> <note>original: *corpore lunari*; "Luna" is the alchemical name for silver</note>. For the gold, you spend 20 to 24 days before you destroy it by means of reverberation. But for the silver, this happens in eight to nine days.

℞. <note>The "Recipe" or "Prescription" symbol.</note> Take 12 <term>Loth</term> <note>a historical German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 14–17 grams</note> of the Body of gold, 8 Loth of the Body of silver, 6 Loth of the Spirit of both <term>in equal parts</term> <note>original: *ana*; a term used in prescriptions to indicate equal quantities of the preceding ingredients</note>, 6 Loth of the Soul of gold, and 4 Loth of the Soul of silver. Place the composition into a glass. <term>Seal it with the seal of Hermes</term> <note>original: *Sigilla Hermetis sigillo*; to hermetically seal a glass vessel by melting the neck closed</note>. Place it in an <term>athanor furnace</term> <note>original: *furnum Athenoris*; a furnace designed to provide steady, continuous heat over many weeks</note> with the weight of two coals, and the fire of the first degree <note>the gentlest level of heat, roughly equivalent to the warmth of a human body or a brooding hen</note>. In this heat it should stand for 30 natural days; thus it enters into putrefaction and the blackness, which is called the <term>crow’s head</term> <note>original: *caput corvi*; also known as "nigredo," this stage of blackness signifies the successful breakdown and "death" of the materials before they can be reborn as the Elixir</note>. This blackness is a sign of the correct procedure in the work. Leave it in such a state for 9 days. Then increase the fire <term>to the weight of one</term> <note>original: *ad pondus unius*; likely referring to a specific measurement of charcoal or a standardized increase in heat intensity</note>

* This royal process might find admirers if gold and silver were not so expensive. ### The best practice for a Philosopher’s Stone, of which one part colors seventeen parts.

Know also that the receiver should have a tube at the back, through which the spirits pass into another...

* It would be a sign of gratitude to us if someone, having learned the truth of this power through us, would wish to provide us with a report of it through the Master Publisher; we request such reports for all of our processes. *Although this process may be good, 
we still do not recognize it as the Great Work 
wherein the writings of our masters agree; 
they had important reasons to withhold 
their secret from the unworthy, even 
through curses. The easier and cheaper the

a single possessor indicates the matter by 
its own name and with complete clarity. 
In other works, we do not heed protestations 
and curses at all, as false goods are sold 
under them just as they are in religion.*

## The Multiplication of the Medicine.

<term>Take</term> <note>original: *℞* (Recipe); the standard symbol for a medical or alchemical prescription</note> <term>mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurium*; common quicksilver</note> and purify it by means of the previously mentioned soot from the stove, with which it is to be placed into a <term>retort</term> <note>a glass vessel with a long, curved neck used for distilling liquids or gases</note> and driven off for <term>augmentation</term> <note>the process of increasing the quantity or potency of the alchemical substance</note>, so that it will finally pass over in a pure state.

Once these 4 <term>Loth</term> <note>a traditional German unit of weight, roughly 15-17 grams or half an ounce</note> have been <term>fixed</term> <note>original: *figiret*; in alchemy, to "fix" a substance is to transform it from a volatile or liquid state into a solid, stable form that can withstand fire</note> in this manner, add another 4 Loth and let them stand in the <term>ashes</term> <note>original: *Aschen*; an ash bath, providing gentle and even heat for laboratory vessels</note> as described above. ### Projection <note>original: *Projectio*; the final, climactic step of the Great Work, where a small amount of the Medicine is "projected" onto molten base metal to transform it into a precious metal</note>

<term>Take</term> <note>original: *℞*, the traditional medical and alchemical symbol for *Recipe*</note> copper and tin, an equal amount of each. Let them melt in a <term>crucible</term> <note>original: *Tiegel*; a ceramic or graphite container for melting metals at high heat</note>, and do this 2 or 3 times. Then apply one part of the Medicine to 17 parts of this prepared and mixed metal. Let it flow for a short time, and pour it into an <term>ingot mold</term> <note>original: *Inguß*</note>. If your work is then brittle, bring it to the <term>cupel</term> <note>original: *Test*; a shallow, porous dish used in assaying to separate precious metals from base metals like lead</note>. If not, it is not necessary to <term>refine</term> <note>original: *abzutreiben*; the process of cupellation, using lead to carry away impurities</note> it with lead. If your work is very brittle, it requires more of the <term>body</term> <note>original: *Körper*; the physical metallic base</note>, namely, of the copper and tin mixed and melted together, which should be added to the Medicine until it is sufficient. Praised be **GOD**.

Place <note>continuing from the previous page: "Place where..."</note> the Most Illustrious <note>original: *Illustrissimi*; a Latin title for a high-ranking noble or person of great distinction</note> was received with his credential <note>original: *Creditiv*; a formal letter of introduction or authorization</note>, who showed it to him in the house of the Pastor at Aßling, which estate lies near Linz <note>a city in Upper Austria</note>. ->In the name of the Lord. Amen.<-

That method <note>original: *Modus*</note> of preparing the <term>First Matter</term> <note>original: *erste Materie*; the base substance required to begin the alchemical Great Work</note> is, in all respects, like yours. However, the way and manner of separating the <term>Sulfur</term> <note>in alchemy, Sulfur represents the active, masculine principle or "soul" of a substance</note> from the <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurio*; the passive, feminine principle or "spirit" of a substance</note>, joining them again, and producing the <term>Medicine</term> <note>the potent substance used for healing or transformation</note>, is as follows:

<term>Take</term> <note>original: *℞*, the symbol for *Recipe*</note>. Of the First Matter, as much as you wish. Break it apart and pour a distilled vinegar over it—enough so that it rises 5 finger-widths above it. Place the vessel in a <term>water bath</term> <note>original: *B. M.*, abbreviation for *Balneum Mariae*; a gentle heating method using a container of water</note> and let it stand therein for eight days. Take the vessel out; the vinegar will then be colored green. Put this aside <term>by decanting and filtering</term> <note>original: *decantando & filtrando*; pouring off the liquid and passing it through a filter</note>. Pour another vinegar onto the material and let the vessel stand for one day in the water bath or on warm ashes, shaking it frequently; then you will see how the <term>Mercury</term> <note>the alchemical spirit</note> is separated from the Sulfur in the form of a foliated <note>leaf-like or layered</note> silver. Put this extract aside again. Do this as often as necessary until you have separated all the Mercury from the Sulfur.

### Augmentation of this Tincture.

...[has] been [made]; then take again a certain weight of common mercury <note>original: *Mercurii vulgi*</note>, and join them <note>original: *conjungire*; to chemically unite two substances</note>, as before. If you see that once again a medicine <note>In alchemy, "medicine" refers to the potent tincture or the Stone itself used for transformation</note> has come of it; then add some of the common mercury again, as above, until you see that the medicine has been transformed into a metal.

However, if you wish to operate by way of an augmentation <note>original: *Augment*; the process of increasing the quantity or potency of the alchemical substance</note>; then take half the weight of your Stone <note>original: *Lapidis*</note> in common mercury. Join it with the same. Place it in the ashes <note>an ash-bath used to provide gentle, even heat</note> over a lamp-fire for two or three days. When you see that the mercury <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> no longer rises <note>referring to the process of sublimation where the substance vaporizes and settles</note>; then join a new portion of mercury to it once more, and proceed as before. Note. <note>original: *NB*</note> When the stove soot—used to distill a solvent <note>original: *menstruum*; a liquid used to dissolve or extract a solid</note> with spirits of wine <note>original: *Weingeist*; high-proof alcohol or ethanol</note>—is placed into a glass vessel, the stove soot should not be added as a powder, but rather in small pieces. It should also be distilled gradually with a gentle fire; otherwise, all the stove soot would rise at once, clump together like a lump in the neck of the vessel, and the glass would break, as happened to me.

Be cautious. Yet the labors are not as difficult as they seem upon reading.

->Matthias<-

<meta>This page transitions from practical alchemical recipes for silver to a philosophical and political defense of publishing these secrets. The author argues against the "royal prerogative" that would keep such knowledge restricted to the nobility.</meta>

<page-num>109</page-num>
<header>First Collection.</header>

## Matthias Herwardt, Organist in Bulfano. <note>Likely referring to Bolzano (Bozen) in modern-day Italy; Matthias Herwardt was a known 17th-century author of alchemical processes.</note>

Many will perhaps think that I have acted wrongly in publishing these and other processes, should they be found to be effective. This is because great lords make a **royal prerogative** <note>original: *Regale*; a sovereign right or monopoly held by a ruler, such as the right to mine precious metals</note> of such works, and would thus rob the children of the art <note>A traditional term for alchemists and practitioners of the "Great Work"</note> of the means required to devote themselves, as they have until now, to the wisdom of **GOD** in investigating His works, for His praise and the benefit of the human race. However, since a great lord who commissions work in this art declares himself by that very act to be a son of the art, he must submit to the law of the fathers and the ancient possessors. By virtue of this law, he cannot forbid such works to those who, like him, have received understanding, wisdom, and skill from **GOD** to undertake such labors. To make a **royal prerogative** <note>original: *Regale*</note> of it would withdraw the blessing of **GOD**, which was intended through these secrets. The authorities should guide and prevent fraud. However, they would act against the temporal welfare of their subjects if they wished to forbid the practice of the arts <note>original: *Praxin der Künste*</note> to intelligent and skilled men whose primary aim is directed toward the promotion of the honor of **GOD** and the welfare of their neighbor. Such a prohibition, due to the suppression of their freedom, has from of old necessarily brought more harm than benefit. For then the owners hid themselves with their treasures, and the seekers—who were partly deceived themselves, partly deceived by others, or were outright fraudsters—inflicted damage upon themselves and others. This turned an art, which is excellent in itself, into an object of mockery among those whose knowledge does not extend far beyond that of the lower and higher schools <note>Referring to the standard education of the time, from basic schooling to university</note>. Is not

<meta>catchword: ein</meta>

<summary>This page serves as a preface to the works of Matthias Herwardt, defending the publication of alchemical secrets against those who believe such knowledge should be a state-controlled monopoly. The author argues that keeping the "art" secret leads to fraud and prevents the fulfillment of God's blessing for humanity.</summary>

<keywords>Matthias Herwardt, Bolzano, royal prerogative, alchemical secrecy, freedom of art, fraud, divine wisdom, public benefit</keywords>

--- Page 136 ---
<meta>This page continues the philosophical justification from page 109, transitioning from the defense of alchemical freedom to a specific practical recipe. The author shifts from a moral warning against greed to the technical "Process 8," credited to Heinrich Grieninger.</meta>

<page-num>110</page-num>
<header>Chemical Experiments</header>

Each person is a gold-maker in his own profession! To this end, one person takes up this work, and another takes up that. What succeeds for one fails for another. Through loss, one finally becomes wise, by grasping better and more certain means to make gold and silver. Since our adepts <note>original: *Artisten*; a term for practitioners of the "art" of alchemy</note> compare their arts to a dangerous sea, we warn all those who have easier and more certain means of providing for themselves and their families in a modest way. However, those driven by greed for money and fame shall either gain nothing, or possess what they have to their physical and spiritual detriment. For such people are an abomination before **GOD** and a laughingstock to the civilized world. Let everyone read our processes as they would those polemical writings on religion, where one judges whether someone has hit upon the truth or not. But he who is unfit for such judgment should remain silent and let others speak.

### 8.

->**A Tincture <note>A potent substance used in transmutation</note> to change Mercury <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> into Silver <note>original: *Lunam* (the Moon); the traditional alchemical name for silver</note>, which Heinrich Grieninger discovered in Burkhausen during wartime in the year 1505.**<-

Make a strong acid <note>original: *Aquafort* (from *aqua fortis*); typically nitric acid</note> from one part saltpeter <note>original: *Nitri*</note>, two parts cinnabar <note>original: *Zinnober*; mercury ore</note>, and three parts alum. ### 10.

## The Great and Wonderful Work of Lord Cardinal Gilbert.

**Take:** Feathers <note>original: *Feder*; likely referring to "feather-alum" or a similar plumose mineral</note>, alum, Roman vitriol, and saltpeter, of each, 1 pound. Pulverize and put them together into an **alembic** <note>A traditional distillation apparatus consisting of a gourd-like vessel and a cooling lid</note>, which is to be sealed with a **lute of wisdom** <term>Lute of Wisdom (*luto Sapientiæ*): a specialized airtight sealing paste made of clay, lime, and other materials, used to prevent the escape of "spirits" or vapors during heating</term>. Then quickly take the second receiver away and put a third glass in its place, which must be strongly **luted** <note>sealed with paste</note>. Let it distill until the end, and indeed, with a strong fire. After this, take this third glass away and preserve it well against evaporation <note>original: *Verriechen*; literally "smelling away," meaning the loss of volatile vapors</note>. For within it is **our penetrating, separating, and dissolving water** <note>original Latin: *aqua nostra penetrativa, separativa & solutiva*</note>, in which all the treasures of the world are hidden, whose countless powers are unknown, and which penetrates and **dissolves** <note>original: *solviret*</note> all metals.

### Projection <term>Projection (*Projectio*): The final stage of the alchemical process, where the "Stone" or powder is cast upon a base metal to transmute it into gold or silver</term>.

Put ten ounces of well-purified **mercury** <note>original: *Queksilber*</note> into a crucible. Place it on a furnace over coals. Blow [the bellows] slowly until the **mercury** <note>original Latin: *Mercurius*</note> becomes quite warm. Then add one ounce of the aforementioned **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original Latin: *Lapid. Philos.*</note> to it; it will melt, penetrate all parts of the mercury, and change it into pure silver that stands up to all tests. In this process, the body is made into a spirit and the spirit into a body, and each sustains the other in the struggle of the fire.

* It should be a joy to a wise man to see the value of gold and silver diminished, and that of wisdom and virtue increased. If God’s people of yesterday were to have a special glory for their wisdom above all other nations, why not today's as well? Yet one still sees servants on horses, and princes walking on foot like servants <note>A reference to the biblical book of Ecclesiastes 10:7, lamenting the reversal of the natural or social order</note>.

In this process, the body is made into a spirit and the spirit into a body, and one maintains the other in the conflict of the fire.

# First Collection

After you have well united these *mercuries* <note>original: *Mercurios*; in alchemy, these are often volatile metallic essences or specialized liquid states of metals.</note> with one another; then add them to the <term>medicine</term> <note>original: *Medicin*; here referring to the alchemical substance or "elixir" being prepared.</note>, and <term>imbibe</term> <note>original: *imbibire*; to saturate or soak a dry substance with a liquid until it can hold no more.</note> it with the <term>water</term> made from <term>egg white</term> and <term>sal ammoniac</term> <note>original: *Salmiac*; ammonium chloride.</note> described below. Then place it in a very gentle <term>fire</term>; thus *under the governance of the fire* <note>original: *ad regimen ignis*; a specific stage in the alchemical process where the intensity of heat is carefully managed to produce change.</note> you will see the signs: a tree which grows with various colors <note>Likely referring to the "Philosophical Tree" or "Arbor Dianae," a crystalline formation of silver that resembles a branching plant.</note>. So *continue* the fire until the whole has descended to the bottom of the glass as a *fixed* <note>original: *fix*; a substance that has become stable under heat and no longer evaporates or changes state.</note> substance. Take this *fixed* material out and add a little <term>urine salt</term> <note>original: *Urinsalz*; salt derived from concentrated urine, valued for its ammonia content.</note> to it. Grind it well and place it for 15 days in <term>horse manure</term> <note>original: *Roßmist*; used as a natural heat source to provide a steady, low temperature for "digestion" or fermentation.</note>; so it will be completely *dissolved*. *Coagulate* <note>original: *coagulire*; to thicken or solidify a liquid through evaporation or cooling.</note> this *solution*. After that, let it flow *by deliquescence* <note>original: *per deliquium*; the process where a solid substance absorbs enough moisture from the air to dissolve into a liquid.</note> upon a piece of marble, which happens quickly. *Congeal* <note>original: *congelire*</note> it again. Thus the power of the <term>medicine</term> is improved *tenfold* <note>original: *in decuplo*</note> up to 60 times in every *solution* and *congealing*.

The <term>egg water</term> for this is made as follows:

℞. <note>Take</note> One part of the white of hard-boiled eggs and 4 ounces of <term>sal ammoniac</term>. Grind them well together and place them for eight days in <term>horse manure</term>. Then take out the glass and <term>distill</term> a water *by means of an alembic* <note>original: *per alembicum*; a classic distillation head or apparatus.</note> with a gentle <term>fire</term>.

### 15.

## All the Secrecy and Art of True Alchemy, together with the Work for the Red and White Tinctures.

<margin>℞.</margin> Take one *loth* <note>a historical German unit of weight, approximately 14–17 grams or half an ounce</note> of vitriol <note>a sulfate mineral, likely iron or copper sulfate</note>, one *loth* of saltpeter <note>potassium nitrate</note>, and pound them both together into a powder. To this, add 1 *loth* of mercury <note>original: *Queksilber*</note>, and mix it well together so that the mercury can no longer be seen. Put it together into a glass flask <note>original: *Kolbenglas*; a round-bottomed flask with a long neck</note>, and place it in hot sand; thus the mercury rises above itself and attaches itself to the top of the glass like white snow. This sublimate <note>the solid substance formed by a gas cooling</note> is called "Mercury" by us.

When you wish to make this mercury more powerful, mix it with its dregs <note>original: *Feces*; the solid waste or residue remaining at the bottom after distillation</note> which remained at the bottom, and take one *loth* of vitriol and one *loth* of saltpeter; mix it all together. Sublimate this once more; thus the mercury becomes stronger.

Take one *loth* <note>a historical weight, roughly 15 grams</note> of mercury <note>original: *Mercurii*</note>, and one *quintlein* <note>about 4 grams</note> of the calx <note>original: *Kalk*; a metallic oxide produced by heating</note> of a common metal. One should know that there are three things with which the transformation <note>original: *Verwandlung* or transmutation</note> occurs: Body, Soul, and Spirit. These two <note>Soul and Spirit</note> are contained within the Body. The spirit is refined from the soul, and the soul is refined from the body, of which it is the solution <note>original: *Solution*; the liquid state of a dissolved solid</note>. The soul transforms and tinges <note>original: *tingiret*; to permeate with color or transformative power</note> the body, which retains the color when they become fixed <note>original: *fix*; stable under heat</note> together.

The philosophers called the body "ashes" <note>original: *Corpus cineres*</note>, the spirit "quicksilver" and "water" <note>original: *Spiritum argentum vivum und aquam*</note>, and the soul "salt" <note>original: *animam*</note>. They have called the spirit "mercury" and the soul "salt." For when the salt is dissolved <note>original: *solviret*</note>, it is also a spirit, because it enters into, brings to life, and illuminates the matters.

Two things which are not of the same nature do not unite. For one thing delights in another when that thing is of its own kind.

For the **Tincture**, things must be metallic by origin and nature; otherwise, they cannot unite together, and neither can make the other perfect.

The second necessary point is this: The **Medicine** <note>In alchemy, the "Medicine" refers to the Philosopher’s Stone or a potent substance capable of curing "sick" (base) metals and transforming them into gold</note> should have its origin in a more powerful metal rather than from a base, inferior metal.

# First Collection

When you wish to unite an imperfect metal with a fine silver, you surely know that the metals must pass through the **fire of the philosophers** <note>original: *Feuer der Philosophen*</note>; that is, through **strong water** <term>Aquafort</term> <note>Nitric acid, a powerful solvent used to dissolve and "purify" metals</note>. In this way, the union occurs through **dissolution** <term>Solution</term> and **solidification** <term>Coagulation</term> in a single process.

**Take** <note>℞</note> one **Loth** <note>a historical German unit of weight, roughly 15-17 grams</note> of silver sand, and one **Quintlein** <note>a smaller unit, roughly 4 grams</note> of sand from an imperfect metal. Make the mixture on a rubbing stone, and let it pass through the **strong water** <term>Aquafort</term>. When it has dissolved, draw it off **by means of an alembic** <term>per alembicum</term> <note>a pear-shaped distilling apparatus</note>, and put it into a glass which is to be sealed with the **seal of wisdom** <term>luto sapientiæ</term> <note>an airtight sealant made of clay, lime, and other materials used to prevent the escape of "spirits" during heating</note>. Place it over a gentle fire, and afterwards provide a strong fire. If any of it rises <note>sublimates</note>, rub it back down into the **dregs** <term>feces</term> <note>the solid residue at the bottom of the vessel</note>. When it no longer rises, it is **fixed** <term>fix</term> <note>it has reached a stable state where it no longer evaporates or changes under heat</note>.

Up to this point, you have learned from a master:
1) that the metals have many names, yet they all point toward a single one;
2) what **mercury** <term>Mercurius</term> is;
3) how one learns to know **mercury**;
4) how the **mercury of the philosophers** <term>Mercurius Philosophorum</term> is to be recognized;
5) what the **silver of the philosophers** <term>argentum Philosophorum</term> is;
6) how one recognizes the **soul** <term>animam</term>, the **spirit** <term>spiritum</term>, and **quicksilver** <term>argentum vivum</term>;
7) how many names the **body** <term>corpus</term> has;
8) how its origin is to be recognized;
9) how you shall give the **body** another nature;
10) how the nature of the body is to be changed;

And the strength present in the treatment, or in the manner of **calcining** <term>Calciniren</term> <note>heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder</note>, is a subtle and agile **refinement** <note>original: *Finanz*; here referring to a sophisticated chemical artifice or result</note> in **quicksilver** <note>original Latin: *argento vivo*</note>, the spiritual kind. The cause of this is the three parts <note>likely referring to the three alchemical principles: sulfur, mercury, and salt</note> and the great heat it possesses, which transforms the **mercury** <term>Mercurium</term> into another nature, as the experience of its **coagulation** <term>Congelation</term> <note>the process of thickening or solidifying a liquid</note> reveals. Raw mercury cannot do this, nor can a **lime** <term>Kalk</term> <note>the powdery metallic residue remaining after heating</note> that has not been prepared in this manner.

You need nothing else but **coagulating** and **dissolving** <term>Solviren</term>; do this until you have a true **medicine** through the power of coagulation.

Do not think that the coagulation of transformations comes through **vitriol** <note>sulfate minerals, often used as strong acidic agents</note> and **alum** <note>a specific mineral salt used in dyeing and alchemy</note>. For it comes solely from the lime made from the pure **earth of mercury**, which is water—hot and dry, steadfast, and having remained in the fire <note>the author uses paradoxical elemental descriptions typical of alchemy, where a substance might be called "water" but act "dry"</note>. For there is nothing in the world that has greater power than this thing alone.

The lime I have spoken of is not the primary cause of the mercury coagulating; rather, it is the subtle smoke and **vapor** <note>original: *vapor*</note> which is released from the lime into the mercury. Through its heating, it enters in an invisible way, and according to nature the...

This smoke must be of the nature of Mercury itself. And when Mercury finds its own nature, it transforms, and not otherwise. It then rejoices in its nature, and they remain friendly together <note>this personification describes how substances with chemical affinities combine easily</note>.

It is now time for me to explain how one should **multiply** <term>Multiplication</term> <note>the process of increasing the quantity or potency of the alchemical "medicine"</note> the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <term>Mercurium Philosophorum</term>. Rub the risen Mercury together with the **dregs** <note>original: *fecibus*; the residue remaining at the bottom</note> and sublime it as before, first with a gentle fire and then with a strong fire, until it rises once more. # First Collection

Perform the **sublimation** <note>the process of heating a substance into vapor and then cooling it back into a solid to purify it</note> six times, and in the final iteration, it becomes the **living silver of the Philosophers** <note>original: *argentum vivum Philosophorum*; a refined, "animated" form of mercury essential for the Great Work</note>.

If you wish to **multiply** <note>increase the quantity or strength of the substance</note> it even further, add more **common mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii vulgi*; standard quicksilver</note> to it and **proceed** as has been described. In this manner, one can **multiply to infinity** <note>original: *in infinitum*</note>.

If you do not have **lime** <term>calx</term> <note>a dry powder produced by heating a metal; here, likely silver calx</note>, make lime using the **living silver of the Philosophers**, just as I previously taught you to do with the metal through **dissolution** <note>original: *Solution*</note>.

We call this **living silver of the Philosophers** the **fixed living silver of the Philosophers**. For one may **fix** <term>fixation</term> <note>the alchemical process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire</note> such a substance into silver, or into a true lime or **medicine** <note>the "Philosopher's Stone" or transformative powder</note>. This is because the power, or the salt of the lime, acts like a lye, and the power or the salt is **dissolved** into the water.

**Living silver of the Philosophers** is **fixed** into good silver, which remains stable through all testing, as follows:

Take <note>original: *℞.* or *recipe*</note> one **Loth** <note>a historical unit of weight, roughly 14.6 grams</note> of the **living silver of the Philosophers**. **Sublime** it three times with a third part of **lime** <note>original: *calcis*</note> (which you shall further use, as I have said, for the **multiplication** of the **living silver** of the **mercury**, which is thus **sublimed**, and with 4 parts of the lime). Place it in a glass vessel, first in a gentle fire, which you should strengthen more and more until it is at its strongest at the very end. Then melt this in a **goldsmith's crucible** <note>a heat-resistant container for melting metals</note>; thus you will find good silver.

Then you should have as much good, soft, red, and well-skimmed honey on hand as you deem necessary to **impaste** <note>original: *impastiren*; to mix into a thick paste</note> the aforementioned gold mass with it. However, for the human body, no **method** <term>Modus</term> is more convenient or better than that of the **spirit of wine** <term>Spiritus vini</term> <note>distilled ethanol</note>, by which, as was said above, the most excellent medicine is obtained, which far surpasses all others.

### 17.

## Special Secrets of Nature and Art in Wine.

Every seeker of the high gifts of God should know the nature of the **quintessence** <term>Quintessenz</term> <note>literally the "fifth essence," a pure substance thought to be the life-force or "soul" of a material, beyond the four physical elements</note> of every thing that has a form. He should know that this is the most delicate soul, which is extracted from its body—as the coarse **matter** <term>Materie</term>—and from the excess of the four elements by the most skillful and ingenious **distillation** <term>Destillation</term>. The ancient sages strove, through this art, to prolong human life, to delay the arrival of death, and to reach the end appointed by God. They did not hold the foolish opinion that life could be extended beyond the goal set by God. Everything that has life and dwells in this **valley of tears** <note>original: *Jammerthal*; a common historical metaphor for the earthly world and its hardships</note> must, according to the Scriptures, eventually die. It is also against nature to be able to preserve life forever and indestructible through a destructible **medicine** <term>Arzeneÿ</term>. Nevertheless, it is within the power of nature to preserve life through a medicine that approaches indestructibility, protecting it against diseases until the aforementioned goal is reached. The best **antidote** <term>Antidotum</term> is the **quintessence**, the soul separated from its body, which is neither cold nor warm, neither moist nor dry—qualities which all other mixed things possess. What with this

...**quintessence** <term>Quintessenz</term> is mixed with, that becomes almost indestructible. For it restores and brings back all the strength that was taken from man by the excess of the elements. It makes the soft hard and softens the hard. It makes the thick thin and the thin thick. It makes the lean fat and the fat lean. It cools the hot and heats the cold. It dries the moist and moistens the dry, as it drives away every **complexion** <term>Complexion</term> <note>in historical medicine, "complexion" refers to the specific balance of the four humors—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—within an individual</note> which is harmful to the body. It destroys all injury caused by excess fluids and restores natural warmth. Therefore, according to philosophical and proven writings, nothing can be found for the preservation of human life that is more credible or useful than the quintessence. 

Even though the quintessence takes on the complexion of every thing added to it, it possesses on its own none of the four **qualities** <term>Qualitäten</term> <note>the primary qualities are hot, cold, moist, and dry</note>—neither that of air nor of fire. surpasses all elements that are beneath the heavens. When one administers such a thing to someone, it **tempers** them and makes the body well-disposed. Even though it is not an element, it is nonetheless a tempered thing, which has been purified by the **elements** <term>Elementa</term> and extracted from the **dregs** <term>fecibus</term> <note>original Latin: *fecibus*, meaning the sediment, waste, or impure remains of a substance.</note> of the four elements, which are the primary cause of decay. Although the **quintessence** <term>Quintessenz</term> can be extracted from all things that have a form, it is mostly to be understood as coming from wine and gold, along with other materials. Nevertheless, the **quintessence** of wine surpasses all others in view of its extraordinary subtlety.

### Extraction of the Fifth Essence of Wine.

Take <note>original: ℞, the traditional symbol for "recipe," derived from the Latin *recipe*, meaning "take."</note> the best red wine from <term>Hungary</term>, from <term>Istria</term>, or from <term>Prosecco</term> <note>original: *Prossegg*; referring to the Prosecco region, known even then for its viticulture.</note>, which should be a little sweet, neither thick nor thin, and should not be turning toward vinegar at all. Take as much of it as you wish, and <term>distill</term> it <term>through an alembic</term> <note>original: *per alembicum*; an alembic is a specialized apparatus used for distilling liquids.</note> very gently, so as not to burn its sugar, four times or more, just as one is accustomed to making a <term>brandy</term> <note>original: *Aquavit* or *aqua vitae*, the "water of life."</note>. The more often this is done, the better it is. Whether the <term>spirit</term> <note>original: *Spiritus*; the volatile, alcoholic part of the wine.</note> has been sufficiently distilled can be recognized when it is lit in a silver vessel; if it burns away cleanly and leaves no moisture behind, it is ready. Place this <term>rectified</term> <note>Purified or concentrated through repeated distillation.</note> <term>spirit of wine</term> <note>original: *Spiritum vini*.</note> into a <term>Pelican</term> <note>A specialized alchemical flask with two side arms that cycle vapors back into the body, used for "circulation."</note>, which is called the <term>vessel of Hermes</term> <note>original: *vas Hermetis*; named after Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy.</note>. This should have a hole at the top, through which one puts the matter to be <term>circulated</term> <note>The process of continuous distillation and condensation in a closed vessel to "refine" the spirit.</note> in and out. When the matter in the vessel—which must be well <term>sealed with lute</term> <note>original: *verlutiren*; to seal a vessel airtight using a paste or cement called "lute" to prevent vapors from escaping.</note> to prevent evaporation—has been transformed into a true <term>Quintessence</term> through a manifold rising and falling; then, in this way, even though it contains the four elements within it, it has passed from destructibility almost into an indestruc-

### How one should separate the four elements.

Take all the waters together which you have drawn off from the wine in order to obtain its Quintessence. M.* or *Balneum Mariae*; a gentle heating method using a container of hot water to surround the vessel, named after the legendary alchemist Mary the Jewess.</note>. Distill it **through an alembic** <note>original: *per alembicum*; an alembic is the "cap" or apparatus placed on top of a boiling vessel to collect and cool vapors.</note>. Pour what has passed over back onto the **residue** <note>original: *feces*; the solid dregs or sediment remaining after distillation.</note>, digest, and distill again. The more often you do this, the better it becomes. What passes over in the seventh distillation is called **Human Blood** <note>This is a metaphorical alchemical name for the "Air" element separated during this stage.</note>, which is the Air, just as the Quintessence is the Water. You therefore have two elevated elements, namely Air and Water.

When you see drops coming like a clear oil, remove the **receiver** <note>original: *Recipienten*; the vessel positioned to catch the distilled liquid.</note> and put a larger one in its place. Do not make a strong fire for 24 hours, otherwise the black matter would rise up into the receiver. But finally, when no more of the oil appears, the fire is to be strengthened until all drops have passed out of the **alembic**. This oil is the element Fire.

The black, dry, and burnt matter lying at the bottom of the **distillation vessel** <note>original: *Destillatorii*.</note> is the Earth, which stinks from the burning,

...[if you] sealed [it] well and placed it in a lime kiln for as long as one usually burns lime, which takes about 14 days. In this way, you would be spared much cost and effort. This is truly the Stone, and not salt, as some say. It is the **rectified philosopher’s stone** <note>original: *lapis philosophorum rectificatus*</note> and the <term>quintessence</term> <note>original: *Q. E.* or *Quinta Essentia*; the fifth element, a pure substance that transcends the four physical elements.</note> which is separated from its four elements, and within which a spirit is hidden. This spirit has been separated from its <term>phlegm</term> <note>original: *phlegmate*; the watery, tasteless residue of distillation.</note> (or water), from its oil (as the air), from the fiery water (which is actually the water element), and from the burnt <term>dregs</term> <note>original: *fecibus*; the solid sediment or "faeces" remaining after heating.</note> (as the element of Earth). For it possesses the highest power of spirituality and the lowest power of corporality. 

Therefore <term>Hermes</term> <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the foundation of alchemy is attributed.</note> says: that which is above is that which is below, and that which is below is that which is above, and it is a single thing, to make gold perfect and to dissolve all other kinds of metals, because it always retains its power and comprises all substances of the four elements in one being, in which lies the secret of all nature's secrets. 

For this reason, the Master says: the stone ascends from the four destructible qualities to heaven, which is indestructible, and it descends again from heaven to the earth. This is precisely what was indicated by the words: *That which is above is that which is below, and that which is below is that which is above.*

### The Governance of Fire in this Work.

Whatever it may be from which you wish to extract the quintessence through distillation, you must first of all <term>putrefy</term> <note>allow the substance to rot or decompose to break down its structure.</note> it in a **water bath** <note>original: *B.M.* or *Balneum Mariae*; a vessel of water used to provide gentle, even heat.</note> and <term>digest</term> <note>to heat gently over a long period to encourage chemical change.</note> it for two months, in order to separate the pure from the impure, and the coarse from the subtle. This digestion should last for one month between the first and second distillation, three weeks between the second and third, 15 days between the third and fourth, 8 days between the fourth and fifth, 4 days between the fifth and sixth, and 2 days between the sixth and seventh distillation. The water bath should always be at a birthing <note>original: *gebährenden*; referring to a gentle, incubation-like heat similar to a womb.</note> and <term>graduating</term> <note>original: *gradirenden*; heat that increases in intensity to concentrate or "grade" the substance.</note> heat. ...violent suffering without a destruction of its primary essence. There are 4 degrees of heat, just as there are 4 <term>Complexions</term> <note>original: *Complexiones*; in humoral theory, a "complexion" or temperament was the balance of the four primary qualities—hot, cold, wet, and dry—found in a substance or person.</note>. The first degree is lukewarm, like water that has been heated in such a way that it does not burn anyone, even if they hold a finger or a hand in it. The second degree is moderately hot, such that it is almost harmless to any limb placed within it. The third degree is nearly boiling hot, unbearable, and not without damage to any limb that one might put into it. The fourth degree is of such a swift heat that one cannot endure it in any way, as it is altogether boiling and exceedingly hot.

The philosophers <note>Alchemists often referred to themselves as "philosophers," and their work as "chemical philosophy."</note> call the fire of the first degree a **fire of the bath** <note>original: *Feuer des Bads*; often called a *balneum Mariae* or bain-marie, using water to provide gentle, even heat.</note>; the fire of the second degree a **fire of the ash** <note>original: *Feuer der Asche*; a sand or ash bath used to provide a higher, indirect heat than water.</note>; the fire of the third degree a **burning fire**. They all reject the fourth degree. 

We therefore warn every lover of this art <note>the "Art" is Alchemy</note> that in each and every <term>distillation</term> <note>original: *Destillation*; the process of purifying a liquid by heating it into vapor and cooling it back into liquid.</note> he should never increase the fire to the fourth degree, because the matter would be ruined by such great heat. Just as with <term>distillation</term>, the fire of the fourth degree would also destroy the quintessence <term>Q. Therefore, no one should dare to attempt this highly respectable art—which has been granted by God for the preservation of human life—unless he has a perfect knowledge of these 4 degrees, as well as the fire in all

In the sixth, it should decrease to one degree and one-quarter of a degree. In the seventh distillation, one should soften the fire so much that it is brought to the first degree, at which it is lukewarm. One must not descend any further than this.

## Gold is *extracted* for this work as follows:

℞ <note>The symbol for *Recipe*, meaning "Take."</note> of the best washed gold or a Hungarian gold, as much as you wish, and three times as much fresh *antimony* <term>Antimonium</term> <note>A metallic element used in alchemy to purify gold, as it reacts with and removes other base metals.</note> that has not been used in any previous work. Furthermore, pour everything into a clean <term>casting cone</term> <note>original: *Gießpukel*; a conical mold into which molten metal is poured so that the heavier purified metal (the regulus) settles at the point.</note> that has been [greased] with <term>tallow</term> <note>original: *Unschlitt*; rendered animal fat used to coat molds to prevent the molten metal from sticking.</note>

...in which it should stand for 24 hours. After the gold has settled, add distilled spring water to it, which should be warm; in this way, the honey separates from the gold. This distillation must happen a second and third time, so that the honey is entirely separated from the gold. Afterward, <term>reverberate</term> <note>original: *reverberire*; to heat a substance in a furnace where the flame is reflected or "reverberated" onto the material, usually to oxidize or further refine it.</note> the gold with a flame fire very gently for three hours in a glass, and let it cool; thus it is sufficiently prepared.

Put this gold into a cucurbit, and pour enough of our rectified <term>water of life</term> <note>original: *Aqua vitæ*; a term for high-proof distilled alcohol or spirit of wine, used here as a powerful solvent.</note> over it so that it rises 6 fingers' breadth above it. Place a <term>blind head</term> <note>original: *blinden Helm*; a distillation cap or "alembic" without a spout, used for refluxing or "digestion" where the vapors are meant to condense and fall back into the vessel rather than being collected.</note> upon it. Let it stand for 4 weeks in <term>digestion</term> <note>original: *Digestion*; the alchemical process of exposing a substance to gentle, continuous heat to soften it or extract its essence.</note>. Then open the glass and distill it cold, so that the color rises with the spirit; thus the white <term>body</term> <note>original: *corpus*; the physical matter remaining after the "soul" or "tincture" has been extracted.</note> remains lying at the bottom <note>original: *in fundo*</note>. But if the color has not yet been sufficiently drawn out of the gold, pour the spirit over it again as previously mentioned, and let it <term>putrefy</term> <note>original: *putreficiren*; a stage in the alchemical process involving slow chemical change through warmth and moisture, often resulting in a change of color.</note> for 14 days longer; thus the color will be completely separated.

Take the colored spirit of wine <note>original: *Spiritum vini*</note> and distill it in a water bath, so the spirit rises over...

### How one should best seal the glasses for these works.

These works require, in view of the highest subtlety in <term>circulation</term> <note>original: *Circuliren*; a process where a liquid is distilled and then flows back into itself repeatedly</note>, fermentation, and distillation, a particularly strong sealing, so that none of the power may be lost. The glass in which you place something should have a long neck. It may be filled up to a third part with the matter. Then place the neck of the glass—about three finger-widths deep—slowly and with care into a small hole of a furnace, which should be no wider than the neck itself; if it is too large, it must be narrowed with clay beforehand so that it fits the neck. Let the mouth of the glass stand freely in the furnace over glowing coals until the front of the neck is nearly glowing and looks as if it is about to melt. Then one presses the neck together without hesitation and while rotating it, using <term>blacksmith’s tongs</term> <note>original: *Schmidszange*</note> which are glowing hot at the tips and must be ready and waiting. After the neck has been quickly constricted and twisted all around, and thus the glass is completely closed, one gently removes the tongs and draws the fire slowly back, so that the glass cools down little by little, for it would shatter in a sudden cold. In this <term>hermetic sealing</term> <note>original: *hermetischen Verschliessen*; an airtight seal named after Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of alchemy</note>, the iron does no good, and the longer the neck is, the less the matter in the glass has to fear from the heat. To use the glasses thus <term>sealed</term> <note>original: *sigillirte*</note>...

**B.** **Highly rectified spirit of wine.** <note>original: *Spiritum vini rectificatissimum*; highly concentrated alcohol</note> Put this into a **circulation vessel** <note>original: *Circulatorium*; a glass vessel designed so that distilled vapors condense and flow back down into the base, repeatedly cycling the substance</note>. ### 18.

## On the Quintessence, or the Oil of Antimony.

The **Quintessence** <note>the most purified and potent essence of a substance</note> of **Antimony** <note>original: *Antimonii* or *Spiesglas*; a semi-metallic element</note> is called the **Lead of the Philosophers**. Its power is almost indestructible, wonderful, and uncommonly useful. It takes away the pain of wounds and heals them in a miraculous way. When it has been purified for forty days in a well-sealed glass in **horse manure** <note>original: *Roßmist*; used as a natural, steady source of gentle warmth for alchemical digestion</note>, it works secret things. Believe me, even if you were to search through all the writings of the **Philosophers** <note>meaning the ancient alchemists</note> for the art of preparing this philosophical lead—

What our **Quicksilver** <note>original: *Queksilber*; mercury</note> can do, this **Lead** <note>original: *Bley*</note> can also do, in that the **mineral** <note>original: *Minera*</note> is thereby **sublimated** <note>a process where a substance is heated into vapor and then condensed back into a solid, usually to purify it</note> in a red color.

℞. <note>original: *Recipe*; Take</note> one pound of **Antimony** <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: *Spiesglas*; a brittle, silvery-gray metalloid favored by alchemists for its purifying properties</note>, and make it into a fine powder. When this oil is driven from one glass into another for some time in a **Pelican** <term>Pelican</term> <note>a specialized glass circulation vessel with two side-arms that allow condensed vapors to flow back into the base, shaped like the mythical bird</note>; then, like a balsam, it heals wounds, **fistulas** <note>abnormal passages in the body</note>, **ulcers** <note>original: *Geschwäre*</note>, and all old injuries.

Do this three or four times, until the **Mercury** and the **Silver** <note>original: *Luna*</note> sublimate together. If you do this correctly and do not use too much heat, you will lose nothing in weight.

Now take the <term>calx of Silver</term> <note>original: *calces Lunæ*; the fine powder or "ashes" produced by the chemical treatment of silver</note> made in this way and add an equal weight of prepared salt and half as much weight of **Vitriol** <note>a sulfate of iron or copper, often used as a corrosive or solvent</note>. With these two salts, you shall **calcine** <note>heat to a high temperature to reduce to powder</note> the <term>Silver</term> <note>original: *Lunam*</note> again for 24 hours. Then wash the salt away from it with urine. Let the calx become dry.

Next, **imbibe** <note>to saturate or soak a dry substance with a liquid</note> the silver calx with the aforementioned <term>Oil of Antimony</term> <note>original: *Spießglasöl*; the potent liquid extracted from antimony described on the previous page</note>. Let it dry, and reduce it back into a <term>solid body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; the metallic form</note>. NB. <note>original: *Nota Bene*; Note well</note> Observe the color. For you should not soak the calx too much, lest it become too red.

The **Vitriol** is prepared for this purpose as follows:

℞. <note>Take</note> <term>reddened vitriol</term> <note>original: *Vitriolum rubificatum*; vitriol that has been heated until it turns red</note>. ### Oil of Antimony.

℞. <note>original: *Recipe*; Take</note> pulverized **Antimony**. Rub it well with a distilled vinegar and place it for six days in warm ashes until the vinegar is consumed and the matter is quite dry. Then pour vinegar over it again and place it in the ashes until it congeals. Next, take a common prepared salt that is liquid. Add the **Antimony** to it and **sublimate** <note>to heat a substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, a process used for purification</note> for four hours. Increase the fire more and more; thus you will obtain a white sublimate. Place the remaining dregs <note>original: *feces*</note> into a linen sack. Then pour distilled vinegar over the dregs <note>original: *feces*</note> so that it stands three finger-breadths deep over it. Place the glass with it for three hours in warm ashes until the matter settles well at the bottom. Strain the vinegar off, but so that the matter is not too dry. Pour other vinegar over it, as before. Do this four or five times. **Note Well.** <note>original: *NB.* / *Nota Bene*</note> Always swirl the glass well whenever you pour the vinegar over it, and then let it settle well. Then pour all the vinegar together into a glass to obtain the **Q. E.** <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; Quintessence, the purest and most concentrated part of a substance</note> contained within it. For when you draw off the vinegar in a **water bath** <note>original: *B. ### 19.

## Permanent Water, <note>original: *Aqua permanens*</note> or the greatest medicine for healing the diseases of both human and metallic bodies.

### Another Process for Oil of Antimony.

Take: pulverized <term>antimony</term> <note>original: *Antimonium*; a metallic mineral highly valued by alchemists for its supposed ability to purify both metals and the human body</note>. Rub it well with distilled vinegar and place it for six days in warm ashes until the vinegar is consumed and the matter is quite dry. Then pour vinegar over it again and place it in the ashes until it <term>congeals</term> <note>original: *congelire*; to thicken or solidify into a mass</note>. Furthermore, take a common <term>prepared</term> <note>original: *præparirtes*; refers to salt that has been purified or chemically treated for laboratory use</note> salt that is liquid. Mix the <term>antimony</term> into it and <term>sublimate</term> <note>original: *sublimire*; the alchemical process of heating a solid until it becomes vapor and سپس collects as a solid again on a cooler surface</note> for four hours. Increase the fire more and more; thus you will obtain a white <term>sublimate</term>. Put the remaining <term>residue</term> <note>original: *feces*; the solid dregs or "dead head" left over after a chemical reaction</note> into a linen sack. Pour warm water over it until the salt is completely separated from it. Keep what remains in the sack. Then pour distilled vinegar over the <term>residue</term> <note>original: *feces*</note> so that it stands three <term>finger-widths</term> <note>original: *Querfinger*; a traditional unit of measurement, roughly the width of a finger</note> deep above it. Set the glass in warm ashes for three hours until the matter settles well to the bottom. Strain off the vinegar, but in such a way that the matter is not too dry. Pour other vinegar on it as before. Do this four or five times.

**NB.** <note>original: *Nota Bene*; Latin for "Note Well"</note> Always swirl the glass well whenever you pour the vinegar over it, and then let it settle well. Then pour all the vinegar together into one glass to obtain the <term>quintessence</term> <note>original: *Q. E.* / *Quinta Essentia*; the "fifth essence," or the most concentrated and pure medicinal spirit of a substance</note> contained within. For when you draw off the vinegar in a <term>water bath</term> <note>original: *B. M.* / *Balneum Mariae*; a gentle heating method where the vessel sits in hot water rather than over a direct flame</note> with a gentle fire, you will find <term>at the bottom</term> <note>original: *in fundo*</note> the quintessence

...essence of antimony. Do the same with the <term>antimony</term> <note>original: *Antimonio*</note> that has been <term>sublimated</term> <note>original: *sublimiret*; a process where a solid is heated until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used for purification</note> with salt, from which the <term>quinta essentia</term> <note>original: *Q. It helps both internally and externally.

An oil of antimony is also made as follows: Boil the antimony in glowing coals with oil of alum, and it will become very red. <term>Coagulate</term> <note>thickening a liquid into a solid</note>, pulverize, and dissolve it <term>by moisture</term> <note>original: *per deliquium*; a method where a salt-like substance is left in a damp place to absorb water from the air until it turns into a liquid</note> upon a stone; thus it will become a red and <term>fixed</term> <note>original: *fixes*; stable and resistant to the effects of fire</note> oil.

* We have good confidence in these works, and whoever can have the same for similar reasons, let him set his hand to it and let us know whether and to what extent we have rendered a service to him and others with this communication, which, like all others, is just as sincere as it was when it came to us.
