Put each of these parts into a coated flask <note>original: *beschlagenen Kolben*; a glass vessel coated with a protective layer of clay or "lute" to withstand the intense heat of a furnace</note> separately, of which there must be six. Place the first flask in the ashes <note>an ash-bath used for even heating</note> and secure it with a helm <note>the top part of a still</note> and a receiver <note>the vessel that collects the liquid</note>. Whatever passes over from this, divide it into two parts. Put the first part into the second flask; but put the second part into a well-coated flask that is large enough to contain all the distillations <note>original: *Destillationes*</note> of the six flasks. Proceed with all the other flasks just as with this first one: one half from the preceding flask is always poured into the following one, and the other half into the large flask which contains no material and which is designated for the collection of all distillations. Take <note>original: ℟, the symbol for *recipe*</note>. Weigh this cemented silver and put it in a flask that should have a long neck. Pour three times as much of the above fixation water <note>original: *Figirwasser*</note> onto it as the silver weighs. Then place a small flask atop the large flask, whose neck should partially contain the small one, and where one should prevent evaporation <note>original: *das Verriechen*; literally "the smelling away," referring to the loss of volatile essences</note> with a good lute <term>luto; a clay or cement used to seal alchemical vessels</term>. Let it stand for 24 hours, and then place it in an ash bath <term>Aschencapelle; a heat-distributing container filled with ashes, used for gentle, uniform warming</term>. At first, give it such a gentle fire that you can hold your hand between the ashes and the flask. Maintain this degree of fire for 45 days, and let the fire neither increase nor decrease. After 5 or 6 days—often even on the first day—you will see a black lime <note>original: *Kalk*; also known as a calx, the powdery residue left after a metal has been "burnt" or chemically broken down</note> fall to the bottom, which multiplies daily until the end of the 45 days, when it ceases to fall.

Then open the flask, and separate the water gently from the lime by tilting <term>per inclinationem; the process of decanting or pouring off a liquid to leave the sediment behind</term>. Wash this [lime] with warm water. After it has been dried, weigh it. Take three times as much well-purified mercury <term>Mercurium</term> and put it in a large crucible in hot ashes so that it becomes warm. For this same purpose, put your black gold-lime <note>original: *Goldkalk*; despite starting with silver, the text now refers to the residue as "gold-lime," suggesting the intended transformation has begun</note> into another crucible. Then amalgamate <term>Amalgamire; to blend a metal with mercury to form a paste</term> the lime and mercury, and

...incorporate, weighing as much as the lime weighs, of a common and prepared salt. Put everything together into a small sublimation flask <note>a vessel used to heat substances into vapor to be collected as solid deposits</note>, and apply a sublimation fire; thus the mercury <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> will rise up, and a yellow mass will remain at the bottom. Let this mass boil in ordinary water to separate the salt from it. Then reduce it on the cupel <term>Capelle; a porous, shallow dish used in assaying to separate precious metals from base metals through intense heat</term> into a body; in this way, if you have put in one pound of silver, you will receive eight ounces of 24-carat gold, which withstands all tests.

\* These and similar grading works <term>Gradier-Arbeiten; alchemical processes intended to refine or "upgrade" a metal's quality and value</term> reveal the proud ignorance of those who trumpet the impossibility of the transmutation of metals. Many a person would be capable of learning something more if he were not ashamed to admit that he still has much to learn in the world. Such people are usually called pedants <note>original: *Schulfüchse*; literally "school-foxes," a derogatory term for those obsessed with narrow academic rules</note>, who fulfill the authoritative saying: The fathers have told it, and we ourselves shall tell it <note>original Latin: *Narravere Patres, & nos narrabimus ipsi*; implying a dogmatic repetition of old teachings without personal investigation</note>. They cannot see as far beyond the hermetic philosophers <note>practitioners of the alchemical and spiritual arts attributed to Hermes Trismegistus</note> as these have seen beyond them since ancient times, for the philosophers did not find themselves forced to sell the freedom of their intellect for the sake of bread. We see no such significant reasons to elevate the fame of today's learned world so high, as long as true theologians and sharp-sighted naturalists <note>original: *Naturkündiger*; those who study the secrets of the physical world</note> are looked down upon within it, and noble time is wasted on trifles and even follies.

# Chemical Experiments

### 21.

### A good and proper method to fix and grade silver <note>original: *Lunam*; the alchemical name for silver, associated with the moon</note>.

<margin>Take:</margin> Red sulfur, Hungarian vitriol <note>a sulfate mineral, likely copper or iron sulfate</note>, and cinnabar <note>mercury sulfide</note>, one ounce <note>original: *Loth*; a historical unit of weight approximately equal to 15-16 grams</note> of each, and then one and a half ounces of silver sheet. Put it into a vessel. Create <term>S. S. S.</term> <gloss>layer upon layer</gloss> <note>original: *stratum super stratum*; a technique of alternating layers of different substances</note>. Place it in a small wind furnace <note>a furnace designed to draw in air to reach high temperatures</note>. Give it a gentle fire for one day. Clear the small vent hole often so that the smoke has its exit, until it no longer smokes. At that point, strengthen the fire. When it has stood on the fire for a good while after the smoking stops, remove it from the fire; you will then find a beautiful red powder, which is to be laid upon a beautiful and smooth brick and placed in a reverberatory furnace <note>a furnace that reflects or "reverberates" heat onto the material from the roof</note> for 24 hours. Then take a little of it and refine it <note>original: *abtreiben*; to separate precious metals from base metals using lead</note> in the lead. Beat thin sheets from it, and place them into an ordinary aqua fortis <note>literally "strong water"; nitric acid</note>. When this dissolves, reverberate it again. Do this until the aqua fortis no longer attacks the silver. Then refine the silver in lead as is proper. Beat sheets from it, which are to be put into an aqua fortis that has been distilled from one part old bricks and one part saltpeter <note>potassium nitrate</note>. Put this water into a small flask. Leave it and place it with the silver into the ash bath <note>a container of sand or ash used to provide even, indirect heat</note>; in this way, it grades itself into pure gold in 4 hours.

### 22.

## To make a water that coagulates mercury into proven silver.

Take <note>original: ℞; the symbol for 'Recipe'</note> Vitriol <note>a sulfate mineral, usually copper or iron sulfate</note> and saltpeter <note>potassium nitrate</note>, as well as a silver calx <note>silver that has been heated until it turns into a fine powder or oxide</note>, of each <note>original: ana</note> one pound. The first water <note>the first portion of the distillate</note> is not good, but rather the second, which is to be made separately. Of this water, take 10 ounces <note>original: Loth; a historical unit of weight approximately 15-16 grams</note>. Into it, place 4 ounces of well-purified mercury <note>original: Mercurio</note>, which will soon turn to water <note>meaning the liquid mercury will dissolve into the acidic solution</note>. Only then add one and a half ounces of fine and very thinly beaten silver, which will also dissolve <note>original: solviren</note>. Let the solution rest well and settle. Afterward, decant <note>pour off the liquid without disturbing the sediment</note> it into another glass with a long neck. Separate the black dregs <note>original: feces; the solid residue or sediment</note> from it. Place the glass with the water in a window in the air, and cover it so that the sun does not reach it. Let it separate <note>original: Scheide</note> thus for 4 days. ### 27.
### To make a Philosopher's Stone from Gold and Mercury.

<margin>Take:</margin> One <term>Loth</term> <note>A historical German unit of weight, roughly 14.6 grams or half an ounce.</note> of gold beaten thin, and six <term>Loth</term> of <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: "Mercurio"</note> that has been <term>sublimated</term> <note>Purified by heating until it turns to vapor and then solidifying it again.</note> and well purified. Make an <term>amalgam</term> <note>An alloy of mercury with another metal, usually soft or paste-like.</note>, which is then to be <term>sublimated</term> through an <term>alembic</term> <note>A distillation apparatus consisting of a boiling pot and a cooling lid with a delivery tube.</note>. Set the <term>residue</term> <note>original: "feces"; the solid dregs left at the bottom of the vessel after heating.</note> aside, and keep the <term>sublimate</term> <note>The material that rose as vapor and solidified in the upper part of the apparatus.</note> in a separate glass. Grind the <term>residue</term> on a stone. Add to it another six <term>Loth</term> of the <term>Mercury</term> and <term>sublimate</term> as before. Again, keep the <term>sublimate</term> separate. Continue in this way until you have <term>sublimated</term> 24 <term>Loth</term>.

Afterward, grind the <term>residue</term> fine. As before, add new <term>Mercury</term> to it and proceed as you have done until now. Continue in this way until you have 48 <term>Loth</term> of <term>sublimate</term>.

If there is still more powder in the glass, grind it fine. <term>Sublimate</term> it with more <term>Mercury</term>. Do this for as long as it takes until it all rises.

* Here is a <term>doubled mercury</term> <note>original: "Mercurius duplicatus"; mercury that has been "animated" or strengthened by being processed repeatedly with gold.</note>. He who does not know how to <term>animate</term> the <term>Mercury</term> with the gold in such a way that

Proceed in this manner until your entire supply of Mercury, which was animated with one **Loth** <note>A historical German weight, approximately 14.6 grams or half an ounce.</note> of gold, has been turned into a red powder.

Then roast this red powder in a gentle heat until it is **fixed**. <note>original: "figiret"; to "fix" a substance in alchemy is to render it non-volatile, so that it remains solid and does not evaporate when exposed to high heat.</note> To find out whether it is fixed, place a small portion of it on a hot plate. If it smokes, put it back in to cook it even longer; you must continue this until it no longer smokes.

* Others add one part of physical gold to the **animated Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurio animato"; mercury that has been "quickened" or prepared by being joined with gold.</note> as a **ferment** <note>original: "fermentum"; in alchemy, a substance—usually gold or silver—added to the Stone to "teach" it how to transform metals into that specific state.</note>, and in a certain manner create the **weight of nature** <note>original: "pondus naturæ"; a term referring to the specific, balanced proportions of ingredients required by natural law.</note>. * False works with **Mercury** and **Gold** <note>original: "Mercurio" and "Sole"; Sol (the Sun) is the alchemical name for gold.</note> have brought the noble **Alchemy** into particular contempt. Among others, **Batsdorf** <note>A pseudonym for Heinrich von Stein, a 17th-century alchemical author.</note> reports in his ***Ariadne’s Thread*** <note>original: "Filo Ariadnes"; a title referring to the mythological thread used to escape a labyrinth, used here as a metaphor for navigating the "maze" of alchemy.</note> how he fared in this regard. Whoever knows how to compare his **operation** against the true one will find the causes of a bad outcome plain to see. Meanwhile, he, like many other **artists** <note>In this period, "Artist" or "Artificer" specifically referred to a practitioner of alchemy.</note>, was not free from the prejudice of haste, believing that his **secret** <note>original: "Arcanum"</note>—to which we grant preference—was the only one attested to by all true **philosophers**. Certainly, on the other side of the mountain, there are also people who understood things that those on this side, in part, could not believe.

### 28.
## *Work for the White & the Red.*
<note>original: "Opus ad Album & Rubrum"; the "White Work" refers to the creation of silver, and the "Red Work" refers to the creation of gold.</note>

<margin>B.</margin>Fine silver, as much as you please. Place it in a small glass. Pour a **strong water** <note>original: "Aquafort"; likely nitric acid, used here to dissolve the silver.</note> upon it, so that it swims over it. Seal the glass very well with a clean cloth. Let it stand in a warm place for an entire month.

Afterward, take the matter out of the glass. Cleanse it in a glazed bowl with warm water for a long time and as often as necessary, until it is pure. This purity is to be recognized by clean and smooth **pins** <note>original: "Stekelein"; small metal pins used as a testing tool. If the water remained "bitter" or acidic, it would react with the pins or taste sharp.</note>, which are placed upon the matter in the bowl. For as long as one still perceives a bitterness in the water on these pins that are placed within, the matter has not yet been properly cleansed.

You must then dry this pure matter gradually, which is further ground into the finest powder. Add an equal weight of **raw mercury** <note>original: "Mercurio crudo"; untreated quicksilver.</note>, and grind them together on a stone so that they may be mixed and become one thing.

Then place this **composite** <note>original: "Compositum"; the mixture of the dissolved silver and the mercury.</note> into another glass. Again, pour as much **strong water** over it so that it is well covered. Seal the glass with a cloth, as above. Let it stand for a month, as before. Cleanse the matter with warm water, as you did above. Do this so

### 32.

### A True Alchemical Practice of a Cardinal for the White and Red Works, concerning a true Congelation and Fixation of Mercury.

<margin>Take:</margin> Silver, as much as you please <note>original: *q. p.*, abbreviation for the Latin *quantum placet*.</note>. Dissolve it in an **aqua fortis** <note>original: *Aquafort*; literally "strong water," referring here to nitric acid.</note> made from **vitriol** <note>Sulfate minerals.</note> and **saltpeter**, and **amalgamate** it with **mercury**. Then let the mercury gently evaporate away from the **silver calx** <note>original: *Silberkalk*; the powdery residue or oxide left after the metal has been treated.</note>. Add to this silver calx an equal weight of **sal ammoniac** <note>original: *Salmiac*; ammonium chloride.</note>, and rub them together on a marble slab so thoroughly that they appear to be only one body. Repeat this three times; in this way, the silver calx is **calcined** <note>Reduced to powder through heat or chemical action.</note> and becomes **fusible** <note>original: *güßig*; able to be melted or cast.</note>.

Some believe that this calcination is better if the **silver** <note>original: *Luna*; Latin for "the Moon," the alchemical name for silver.</note> is dissolved in the aqua fortis and, after the solvent is **distilled off** <note>original: *Abstraction*.</note>, is **sublimed** <note>Heated until it turns to vapor and solidifies again.</note> with an equal amount of sal ammoniac. This would have to be repeated three times.

When this is done, take three parts of **Salt of Anatron** <note>original: *Salis anatron*; likely natron or soda ash (sodium carbonate).</note> and one and a half parts of silver calx. Let the salt dissolve in a strong **vinegar**, and throw the silver calx into it. Stir it well with a small stick so that they are well incorporated with one another. After this, rub it well on a marble slab and let it **deliquesce** <note>original: *zerfliessen*; to melt or turn into a liquid by absorbing moisture from the air.</note> in a damp place. When it has completely liquefied, filter it through a linen cloth and keep the liquid in a tightly closed glass.

<margin>Take:</margin> one part of **mercury** that has been purified and pressed through leather. Let it

### 33.
### *Potable oil of gold.* <note>original: *Oleum auri potabile*; a legendary medicinal elixir of liquid gold believed by alchemists to cure all ailments and grant longevity.</note>

<margin>Take</margin> gold, as much as you can drink without cutting back on your food <note>This likely refers to the dosage intended for consumption or perhaps the cost of the material relative to one's sustenance</note>. Dissolve it in **aqua regia** <term>original: *Königswasser*; literally "royal water," a mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids capable of dissolving "noble" metals like gold</term>. Distill the **solvent** <note>original: *menstruum*</note> halfway **by means of an alembic** <note>original: *per alembicum*; a pear-shaped glass vessel used for distilling</note>. Set the remainder in a cold place; thus almost all the gold will be transformed into small crystals <note>original: *Steinlein*</note>. Take these out and dissolve them in vinegar that has been distilled three times. Distill this vinegar halfway by means of an alembic. Place the remainder in a cold place again; thus the gold will again be transformed into small crystals. Separate these from the vinegar and dissolve them in common and distilled water. Then distill off all the water by means of an alembic; thus your gold will be perfectly prepared for **solution** <note>the state of being dissolved</note>.

<margin>Take</margin> ten pounds of highly rectified **spirit of wine** <note>original: *Spiritu vini*; concentrated ethanol</note>, and one pound each <note>original: *ana*</note> of common salt and **salt of tartar** <term>original: *Weinsteinsalz*; potassium carbonate, often used as a flux or purifying agent</term>, both of which are to be **prepared** <note>refined for chemical use</note>. Mix them and place them in a **water bath** <term>original: *B. M.* or *Balneum Mariae*; a gentle heating method using a container of hot water</term>, in which they should stand for four days. After that, distill them in a dry and **tempered**

Take eight ounces of this distilled water and one ounce of the gold prepared above. White vinegar should be distilled three times and no more, as it would otherwise lose its power.

If you wish to preserve the **spirits** <note>the volatile, active essence of a substance</note> of any thing, let the beak of the **alembic head** <term>original: *Helm*; the cooling cap of the distilling apparatus</term> reach all the way to the bottom of the **receiving vessel** <term>original: *Recipienten*; the container that collects the distilled liquid</term>.

The **aqua vitae** <term>original: *Aquavit*; literally "water of life," referring to highly concentrated alcohol</term> should be separated from its **watery residue** <term>original: *phlegmate*</term> as thoroughly as possible, and then distilled seven times **by means of an alembic** <note>original: *per alembicum*</note>.

### 34.
## A Universal from Rainwater.

During the greatest thunderstorm, collect the water that falls from the air through a stretched-out cloth into a large and clean **vat** <term>original: *Kufe*; a large open tub or barrel used in brewing and dyeing</term>. The more of it you collect, the better it is. Place it in a clean cellar and cover it diligently, so that neither dust, nor vermin, nor any impurity may fall into it. Do not reach into it with your hands, and prevent either the sun, the moon, or the stars from shining into it.

Leave this water standing covered for at least 10 days and nights. Afterward, open the lid from time to time—yet without moving the vat—to look upon it; thus you will finally perceive that a skin gradually grows upon the water, which then takes on all kinds of colors, which are much more beautiful to behold than those of a rainbow <note>this multicolored stage is often called the "peacock's tail" (*cauda pavonis*) in alchemical literature, signaling a successful transformation</note>. This skin changes further, as it

### 34.
## A Universal from Rainwater.
<term>Universal: A "Universal" or "Universal Medicine" is a substance believed by alchemists to be capable of curing all diseases or perfecting any base metal, often derived from a source containing "world-spirit," such as dew or rain.</term>

During the greatest thunderstorm, collect the water that falls from the air through a stretched-out cloth into a large and clean vat <term>original: *Kufe*; a large wooden tub or vessel</term>. The more you collect of it, the better it is. Place it in a clean cellar and cover it diligently, so that neither dust, nor vermin, nor any impurity may fall into it. Leave this water standing covered <note>original: *unaufgedeket*; literally "not uncovered," meaning it should remain sealed or undisturbed</note> for at least ten days and nights. Afterward, open the lid from time to time, but without moving the vat, to look inside; thus you will finally perceive that a skin gradually grows upon the water, which then takes on all kinds of colors, which are much more beautiful to behold than those of a rainbow. When this water begins to purify itself <note>original: *läutern*; the process of the water clearing as the sediment settles</note> and to lose the stench, one must occasionally reach into it with a clean glass—but without wetting one's hand—scoop a little out, and inspect it in the bright sun to see if it is as clear as any other pure water. Since this water is exceedingly healthy for drinking and does not spoil, you may keep it.

...carried into molten copper, it <term>tinges</term> <note>From the Latin *tingere*; in alchemy, this refers to the process of "coloring" or fundamentally transforming a base metal into a noble one.</note> it into a permanent and fine silver. ℞ <note>The traditional symbol for *Recipe*, meaning "Take."</note>. Take the aforementioned royal, lifeless, and white body. Grind it upon a piece of marble. Give it no greater heat than that produced by old horse manure, or that of a brooding hen <note>Alchemists used biological metaphors to describe specific low-temperature ranges; horse manure and nesting birds provide a steady, gentle warmth of about 30–40°C (86–104°F).</note>. After this, take it out, and you will see that it has become uncommonly beautiful, fixed, transparent, and can even be more beautiful than a ruby.

...cures internal <note>The OCR begins with "che," the suffix of the German *innerliche* (internal).</note> and external diseases, and also transforms gold, when it is cast upon it while in flux, into a substance of its own nature. For one part of it changes a thousand parts of gold into a dark brown-red powder, shaped like mountain cinnabar <note>original: *Bergzinnober*; native cinnabar (mercury sulfide), known for its bright red color.</note>. Of this true <term>tincture</term> <note>original: *Tinctur*; in alchemy, a concentrated substance capable of transmuting base metals into noble ones.</note>, one part colors a thousand parts of all kinds of molten or red-hot metals into the most permanent and best gold.

℞ <note>Take</note>. One <term>grain</term> <note>original: *Gran*; a tiny historical unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 65 milligrams.</note> of the <term>ruby stone</term>. One who is awake should be dried with warm cloths during this. For a complete restoration, some sick persons must be given another half <term>grain</term>. Otherwise, the <term>dosage</term> for those from 7 to 14 years of age is half a grain. From 14 to 100 years, however, it is one grain.

\* This process originates from the <term>middle world</term> <note>original: *Mittelwelt*; a term in alchemical cosmology likely referring to the realm of the soul or the astral plane, situated between the physical earth and the divine heavens.</note>, and can at least draw the attention of those who search for everything beneath and upon the earth.

### How to prepare the Saffron of Mars for this water.

℞ <note>original: ℞; the symbol for "Recipe" or "Take."</note> Iron filings, and heat them in an iron spoon until they glow. Then throw them into a bowl filled with red vinegar. Do this ten times. When they have cooled, grind them. <note>The result is "Crocus Martis" or iron oxide, named for Mars, the Roman god associated with iron.</note>

℞ 6 ounces of the burnt copper, the same amount of <term>Saffron of Mars</term> <note>original: *croco Martis*.</note>, 3 ounces of <term>quicklime</term> <note>original: *lebendigen Kalk*; calcium oxide, a caustic alkaline substance.</note>, and two ounces of <term>sal ammoniac</term> <note>original: *Salmiac*; ammonium chloride, frequently used in alchemy to help dissolve metals or facilitate reactions.</note>. Grind them together well in a mortar until they are like a dough, which occurs through the power of the lime. Place this dough into a flask that is to be fitted with a <term>head</term> <note>An alembic or distillation cap.</note>. <term>Sublimate</term> <note>original: *Sublimire*; the process where a solid is heated until it turns into a gas and then cools back into a solid, used here to purify the mixture.</note> again, as before. Do this three times, and always provide the strongest fire at the end. Then you will have a <term>fixed</term> <note>original: *fixen*; "fixed" refers to a substance that has become stable and will no longer evaporate when heated.</note> sal ammoniac, which has become uncommonly red through the burnt copper and the Saffron of Mars. Place this sal ammoniac in a damp location; thus it will for the most part <term>dissolve</term> <note>original: *solviren*.</note> into a water that is redder than blood. This red water has the power to dissolve, to <term>fix</term> <note>original: *figiren*; to make a substance resistant to the fire.</note>...

#### One part in a hundred for the White Work. <note>original: *ad Album*; refers to the *Albedo* or white stage of the Great Work, intended to produce silver.</note>

<margin>℞.</margin> <note>Take</note> Mineral mercury, and first purify it with vinegar and salt, then with <term>aquavit</term> <note>original: *Aquavit*; "water of life," a term for distilled alcohol or spirits</note> and <term>tartar</term> <note>original: *Weinstein*; potassium bitartrate, often used as a purifying agent.</note>. Then make an <term>aqua fortis</term> <note>original: *Aquafort*; literally "strong water," a powerful corrosive acid like nitric acid.</note> from rock alum and <term>nitre</term> <note>original: *Nitro*; saltpeter.</note>. Of this water take four ounces, but of the sublimate only one, which is to be <term>dissolved</term> <note>original: *solviren*</note> in the water. Place this solution into <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: *Fäule*; a state of gentle, moist heat, often achieved using a warm bath, to encourage chemical breakdown</note> for four days; thus little stones <note>crystals</note> will appear, which are to be properly separated from the aqua fortis.

Afterward, put these little stones into four ounces of aquavit, and <term>distill</term> <note>original: *abstrahire*; to draw off a liquid via heat, leaving the solid behind.</note> it from the matter, which remains black <term>at the bottom</term> <note>original: *in fundo*</note>.

<meta>This page continues a philosophical discussion from page 232 regarding the difficulty of interpreting alchemical texts. The text then transitions into a practical recipe for a "proven art" involving the processing of lead.</meta>

P 5

...and which were set up for various works. The former harmonize in a way obvious to the wise. The latter, however, discord, as they had different <term>subjects</term> <note>original: *Subjecta*; the base materials or primary substances being acted upon</note> and also different <term>methods of proceeding</term> <note>original: *modos procedendi*</note>. The greatest follies have been committed by those who wanted to create harmonies among the writers, whom they understood neither halfway nor at all. As with Holy Scripture, so it also went with the writings of the <term>Adepts</term> <note>original: *Adepten*; master alchemists who have supposedly attained the secret of the Philosopher's Stone</note>. He who did not have the right keys to their understanding had to guess and believe he had hit upon the right meaning.

# First Collection.

...you can reduce a <term>body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; in alchemy, the solid metallic or chemical substance being worked upon</note>, or perform <term>projection</term> <note>original: *Projection*; the final stage of the Great Work where the philosopher's stone is cast upon base metal to transmute it</note> with it. Note that this matter attains an even greater <term>tincture</term> <note>original: *Tinctur*; the transformative essence or power to change the quality of a metal</note> the more it is boiled. *Praise be to GOD* <note>original: *Laus DEO*</note>.

* Our *sharing of knowledge* <note>original: *Communication*</note> should also be pleasing to those <term>laboratory workers</term> <note>original: *Laboranten*; here referring specifically to alchemists or chemical assistants</note> who, in part, seek their bread through intentional fraud. For if they, as good workers, truly understand the manual techniques, then wealthy people will certainly be found who will pay them for their efforts to work this or that out, in such a way that no need will force them to put their honor or even their life in danger. They should only wait until they are called, and then do what they are commanded. Every laborer is worthy of his hire. And when the dishonor that has attached itself to the Art because of fraudsters finally ceases, then one may expect honor and benefit from it. To handle excessive wealth, the shoulders of the intellect and will must be strong in *Christ*, for without this, such wealth always falls harmfully. Therefore, each and every person should be content with the temporal portion that stands in proportion to their status and calling. Spiritual goods alone are our property in *Christ*, and all temporal goods are given to us so that we may be stewards over them, letting the abundance go to those whom *GOD* sends to us in poverty, to see whether we prove our faithfulness through good works or whether, like those *Pharisees*, we would neglect or even mock the many strong exhortations to love one's neighbor out of greed and pride. Certainly, a person of such a character can never hope for the true treasure in *Christ*. *Luke 16, verses 9—15.*

->38.<-

->*The truest art for the White and the Red.*<-
<note>original: *Ad Album & Rubeum ars verissima*; refers to the two main goals of alchemy: the "White Work" to produce silver and the "Red Work" to produce gold.</note>

℞ <note>The traditional symbol for "recipe," meaning "Take."</note>. One ounce of purified <term>mercury</term> <note>original: *Queksilber*</note> that has been pressed through leather. Place this into a large <term>receiver</term> <note>original: *Recipienten*; a glass vessel designed to collect distilled liquids or vapors</note>. Place into a <term>retort</term> <note>original: *Retorte*; a glass flask with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note> one ounce of saltpeter and two ounces of rock alum, which is to be <term>calcined</term> <note>original: *calciniren*; the process of heating a substance to a high temperature to reduce it to a powder or "ash"</note> in the best possible way. Apply a gentle heat, so that the vessel might not break on account of the <term>spirits</term> <note>original: *Geister* or *Spiritus*; the volatile vapors or acidic fumes released during heating</note>. Place the receiver, in which the mercury is kept, into water, and bathe it constantly with a sponge dipped in water; this is because of the strength of the spirits which will distill onto the mercury. Then the mercury will be turned into an oil. Let all the spirits evaporate away from the mercury, and it will become a yellow powder. Proceed with this dry mercury as before; thus it will again become an oil. Keep this along with its spirits, and seal the vessel as best as possible against evaporation <note>original: *Verriechen*; literally "smelling away," referring to the loss of volatile substances through a poor seal</note>.

Then ℞. one ounce of fine silver <term>calcined</term> in <term>aqua fortis</term> <note>original: *Aquafort*; literally "strong water," a historical term for nitric acid</note>. Grind this <term>silver calx</term> <note>original: *Silberkalk*; the powdered residue of silver after it has been treated with acid or heat</note> as finely as possible. Place it in a receiver, as I mentioned above regarding the mercury. On the first day, let the spirits of nitre <note>original: *Spiritus Nitri*; nitric acid vapors</note> from one part nitre and two parts calcined alum pass over in the same way I indicated for the mercury. Project one part of this onto a hundred parts of living mercury; thus they will become a silver that withstands all tests. This work is completed in three months. **Praise be to GOD always.** <note>original: *Laus DEO semper*</note>

### 39.
->A red fixed water.<-

Take: four ounces of native or red sulfur, four ounces of vitriol <note>A sulfate mineral, likely iron or copper sulfate</note> calcined to redness, four ounces of <term>crocus Martis</term> <note>original: *crocum Martis*; literally "Saffron of Mars," a reddish-brown iron oxide used as a pigment and alchemical reagent</note>, and eight ounces of sal ammoniac <note>original: *Salmiac*; ammonium chloride, used as a flux and to react with other minerals</note>. Grind everything well together, and pour enough of the strongest vinegar over it so that the powder becomes quite wet. Place this for four weeks in warm horse manure <note>original: *Roßmist*; decomposing manure was a standard laboratory tool for providing gentle, consistent heat (incubation) over long periods</note>, which is to be renewed every ten days, until the water has become red.

Then take: four ounces of lime made from eggshells, and one ounce of mastic <note>A resin from the mastic tree</note>. Grind them together and put them into the aforementioned red water to be dissolved therein. Put this water into a glass and draw it off <term>by means of an alembic</term> <note>original: *per alembicum*; an early distillation apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube</note>, pouring the water back onto the <term>dregs</term> <note>original: *feces*; the solid residue left after distillation</note>. Do this three times; thus the water will become uncommonly red. Keep this water for yourself, as well as the <term>dregs</term> which remain <term>at the bottom of the vessel</term> <note>original: *in fundo vasis*</note>.

Take: one part of a fixed silver and one part of fine gold. Melt them together. Flatten them into thin sheets. Arrange them <term>layer upon layer</term> <note>original: *S. S. S.*, an abbreviation for *Stratum Super Stratum*</note>. <term>Cement</term> <note>original: *Cementire*; a process of heating metal sheets packed in a powder to purify or color them</note> them. Melt them together; then it will be gold. Flatten this gold into sheets once more. Heat the plates until they glow, and quench them in the aforementioned red water; thus the quality of this gold will be doubled. Continue this quenching until it is enough. When you wish, put mercury into a

...crucible. Let it become a little hot. Then pour four or five drops of the red water <note>The "Red Fixed Water" described in the previous recipe</note> upon it; thus it will become gold. <note>original: *Occulta hoc opus*; Hide this work.</note>

* That which is to be hidden for the sake of misuse, is to be revealed for the sake of proper use. If you wish to abolish the misuse of gold and silver, you must remove these metals from the world entirely. These metals remain in the world even without our arts, and would to God that the value placed upon them might be diminished, while the value of understanding and virtue might be increased. The entire world has become infatuated with these metals; and who is there to judge that through the ancient love in Christ—just as with gold—other temporal goods should also ebb and flow, and thus serve the general welfare? Contrary to this, one sees on one side a hundred people who, through greed, deny the providence of God, and under the pretext of thriftiness, seek to maintain and increase their abundance by every means. On the other side stand a thousand people who must complain of poverty and want, and who, amidst tears and sighs, must attribute their misery to a love that has grown cold among Christians. This cold love is a clear sign that chaste faith, which rests upon a thorough understanding of the teachings of Christ, has erred in the most important matters since ancient times; through this faith, the ancient love shall finally express itself again and bring temporal blessings to all.

distilled vinegar. Filter and <term>coagulate</term> <note>to solidify a liquid into a thickened or solid mass</note> this solution. Then take a water which is to be distilled from the whites of hard-boiled eggs. Pour this onto the alkali to dissolve it. Coagulate this solution once more; thus the salt is prepared.

Now dissolve <term>nitre</term> <note>original: *Nitrum*; saltpeter or potassium nitrate</note> in vinegar or in filtered urine. Then increase the fire a little, and strengthen it more and more over twenty-four hours; thus you will obtain silver.

## Nicolai Vergersperger, a Frankfurter’s Art of changing silver into 24-carat gold.

<sig>B.</sig>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the transmutation of silver into 24-carat gold, a process attributed to Nicolai Vergersperger, following the preparation of the necessary salts on the previous page.</meta>

<page-num>241</page-num>

<header>First Collection</header>

℞ <note>original: *Recipe*; take</note> **C**innabar <note>mercury sulfide, often used for its deep red color</note>, upon which a little aqua fortis <note>nitric acid</note>—which has been distilled from vitriol and nitre and purified with silver—is to be poured. Take equal parts <note>original: *ana*</note> of this cinnabar and common ground salt, and with this mixture, <term>cement</term> <note>a process of heating a metal surrounded by a powdered chemical to alter its properties</note> a laminated silver sheet for ten days, for eight or twelve hours each day, always using fresh powder. Mix two parts of this cemented silver with one part of <term>calx of gold</term> <note>gold that has been reduced to a fine powder or oxide</note>, grind them well together two or three times with a red Venetian wine, and then melt them in a crucible. Beat this mixture into a sheet, which is to be placed into the <term>graduation water</term> <note>original: *Gradirwasser*; a solution used to refine or "graduate" a metal to a higher degree of purity or color</note> described below, and left there for six or seven days over the gentle fire of an <term>ash bath</term> <note>original: *Aschencapelle*; a container filled with ashes to provide indirect, gentle heat</note>; thus one obtains gold. If it has not been <term>tinctured</term> <note>original: *tingiret*; the alchemical process of deep coloring or transmuting a substance</note> to 24 carats the first time, it should be placed into the graduation water again and again until it has been sufficiently tinctured.

->The graduation water is made thus:<-

℞ <note>Take</note> one pound each of <term>calcined</term> <note>heated to a high temperature to remove moisture or volatile parts</note> vitriol and saltpeter, three ounces of cinnabar, and two ounces of verdigris <note>the green pigment or crust that forms on copper</note>. Distill this, and place one ounce of rainwater into the receiver. Of this water, take three parts, and of the <term>Luna</term> <note>original: *Luna*; the alchemical name for silver, associated with the moon</note>—which was composed of silver and gold as described above—take two parts. Lay it in this water for 24 hours. The longer it lies therein, the more it increases in carats.

Then increase the fire a little, and strengthen it more and more over twenty-four hours; thus you will obtain silver.

### 41.

## **The Art of Nicolaus Vergersperger, a citizen of Frankfurt, for Transmuting Silver into 24-Carat Gold.**

<meta>catchword: Tin</meta>

<summary>This page describes the final steps for preparing alchemical salts and mercury to produce silver, followed by the introduction of Nicolaus Vergersperger's method for creating 24-carat gold.</summary>
<keywords>alkali, nitre, distillation, coagulation, deliquium, mercury, silver, gold transmutation, Nicolaus Vergersperger, Frankfurt</keywords>

--- Page 279 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical instructions for transmuting silver into gold. The previous page detailed the preparation of specific salts (nitre and alkali) to be mixed with mercury; this page describes how to use cinnabar and a special "graduating water" to complete the refinement of silver into high-karat gold.</meta>

<page-num>241</page-num>
<header>First Collection.</header>

Take <term>cinnabar</term> <note>a bright red mineral consisting of mercury sulfide</note>, upon which you are to pour a little **<term>aqua fortis</term>** <note>Latin: *aquafort*; "strong water," or nitric acid</note> that has been distilled from **<term>vitriol</term>** <note>sulfate minerals</note> and **<term>nitre</term>** <note>saltpeter or potassium nitrate</note> and purified with silver. Take this <term>cinnabar</term> and common ground salt in **equal parts** <note>original: *ana*</note>, and with it **<term>cement</term>** <note>original: *cementire*; a process of heating a metal surrounded by a powdered chemical mixture to change its properties</note> a laminated silver for ten days, between eight and twelve hours every day, always using fresh powder. Two parts of this **cemented silver** are to be mixed with one part of **<term>gold calx</term>** <note>original: *Goldkalks*; gold that has been reduced to a fine powder through heating or chemical dissolution</note>, rubbed thoroughly together two or three times with a red Venetian wine, and then melted in a **<term>crucible</term>** <note>original: *Tiegel*; a ceramic or metal container used for melting materials at very high temperatures</note>. Hammer this mixture into a **sheet**, which is to be placed into the **<term>graduating water</term>** <note>original: *Gradirwasser*; a chemical solution used to increase the purity or enhance the color of gold</note> described below, and left there for six or seven days over the gentle fire of an **<term>ash bath</term>** <note>original: *Aschencapelle*; a method of indirect heating where a vessel is placed in a container of warm ashes</note>; in this way one obtains gold. If it has not been **<term>tinctured</term>** <note>original: *tingiret*; alchemically transformed or colored</note> to 24 **<term>karats</term>** the first time, it is placed back into the **<term>graduating water</term>** again and for as long as it takes until it is sufficiently **<term>tinctured</term>**.

->The **<term>graduating water</term>** is made thus:<-

Take **<term>calcined vitriol</term>** <note>vitriol heated until it becomes a dry powder</note> and **saltpeter**, **equal parts** <note>original: *ana*</note>, one pound; <term>cinnabar</term>, three **ounces**; and **verdigris** <note>original: *Grünspan*; the green pigment or crust that forms on copper</note>, two **ounces**. **Distill** this, and put one **ounce** of rainwater into the **receiver** <note>original: *Recipienten*; the vessel that collects the liquid after it has been vaporized and condensed</note>. Of this water, take three parts, and of the **<term>silver-gold alloy</term>** <note>original: *Luna*; literally "the Moon," the alchemical symbol for silver, but here referring to the silver and gold mixture composed above</note>, two parts. Place it in this water for 24 hours. The longer it lies therein, however, the more it increases in **karats**.

# Chemical Trials

* Whoever does not know how to add to or take away from a process should leave it alone, so that he does not have to pay a price for his learning <note>original: *Lehrgeld*; literally "apprenticeship money," a German idiom for learning a painful lesson through failure</note> that might cause him and others pain.

### 42.

### The Tincture of Mercury into the Sun, and it is an Elixir.
<note>original: *Tinctura Mercurii in Solem*; in alchemy, the "Sun" is the traditional symbol for gold.</note>

<margin>Take:</margin> <term>Salmiac</term> <note>ammonium chloride</note>, <term>alkali salt</term> <note>original: *Sal alcali*; likely potassium carbonate or a similar alkaline substance</note>, green calcined copper, and <term>saltpeter</term> <note>potassium nitrate</note>, **of each an equal part** <note>original: *ana*</note>. Grind everything thoroughly together on a marble slab. Place it in a well-sealed **urinal** <note>a glass vessel shaped like a flask, used here for chemical reactions</note> in a damp place, and let it stand there until the matter becomes moist. After that, distill it **by means of an alembic** <note>original: *per alembicum*; a chemical still used for distilling liquids</note>. Throw away the first water. The second is good. The third should be kept as a treasure. Place the **mercury** into the water; thus it will be **calcined** <note>reduced to a powder through heat or chemical action</note> like gold. Then **distill off** <note>original: *abstrahire*</note> this water; it is then called **mercury water** <note>original: *aqua Mercurii*</note>.

Then take: a green **tutty** <note>original: *Tutiam*; a crude zinc oxide collected from the flues of smelting furnaces</note>. Place it in a covered or sealed crucible. Put it into a glass-furnace to **calcine** <note>burn to a powder</note> for 24 hours. Grind the matter to a powder on a stone. **Saturate** <note>original: *Imbibire*; the process of repeatedly soaking a dry substance with liquid and drying it</note> this with the mercury water mentioned above, until it becomes like a salt. Dry this in the sun, or over the gentle fire of an **ash bath**. Grind it again on a marble slab and saturate it as before. This saturation and drying is done five times. Finally, make it into a powder. Project one part of this onto 30 parts of **mercury** that has been purified with vinegar and salt; thus it will be changed into gold.

### 43.

## Obtaining Gold from Copper.

In Bohemia, a metal is found which, according to its color, looks like **antimony** <note>original: *Antimonium*; a brittle, silvery-white semi-metal often used in alchemical purification</note>, and yet it is not antimony. It is called **Saffergail** <note>a local historical name for a mineral, likely related to "zaffre" (cobalt ore) or a specific copper-lead-antimony mineral found in the region</note>, and is found at Nuedberg. With this, gold is obtained from copper as follows:

<term>Take</term> <note>original: *℞.*, the traditional medical and alchemical symbol for *recipe*</note> copper and **calcine** <note>to heat a substance to high temperatures to oxidize it or reduce it to powder</note> it with sulfur. Grind it well and **reverberate** <note>to heat the material in a furnace where the flames are reflected or "reverberated" down onto the substance</note> it for three hours; in this way, the sulfur departs from it. Stir it constantly with an iron rod until you see that it has turned into a reddish color. Meanwhile, use a means to escape the harm of the stench <note>a practical warning about the toxic sulfur dioxide fumes produced during roasting</note>. Grind this calcined copper on a stone. Add new sulfur to it. Calcine, grind, and reverberate as before. Do all of this as many times as necessary until three parts of the copper have vanished, and only the fourth part remains. Then it is enough. Bring this fourth part into a **melt** <note>original: *Fluß*</note> with **alkali salt** <note>original: *sale alcali*; likely potash or soda ash used as a flux to help the metal melt</note>. When it is thus flowing, place it in a vessel called a **refining cupel** <note>original: *Scheidtest*; a porous vessel used in assaying to separate precious metals from base metals</note> in a **blast furnace** <note>original: *Windofen*; a furnace that relies on a strong draft of air to reach high temperatures</note>. ### Sublimation of Sulfur from Mercury <note>original: *Sublimatio Sulphuris a Mercurio*</note>

Grind this matter into a pure powder. Put it into a **vial** <term>Phiole</term> <note>a small glass vessel</note> with a wide neck. However, the neck should not be higher than two fingers' breadth, and the glass should not be filled more than half with the matter. Place the glass on the hole of the upper floor of the furnace. If the glass is heat-resistant, it is not necessary to **lute** <term>lutiren</term> <note>to seal or coat the vessel with a clay paste to protect it from the fire</note> it. But if it is not to be trusted, you may lute it finely and smoothly as far as it stands over the hole. The hole in which the glass stands is to be made such that the fire does not force its way out through the gaps. After that, give it a gentle fire for half an hour. Then strengthen the fire with small and short wood. Give it a **flame fire** <note>a more intense heat where the flames come into closer contact with the vessel</note> for 8 or 10 hours, until the sulfur is almost entirely driven off <note>original: *ausgerochen*, literally "smelled out," referring to the evaporation of sulfurous fumes</note>, and the mercury alone remains in the glass, baked together like a hard bread, and remains reddish in color. Note also that for two ounces of **mercury** <term>Mercurii</term>, no more than an eighth [of an ounce], or for eight **loth** <term>Loth</term> <note>a pre-metric unit of weight; eight loth is approximately four ounces</note> of mercury, only one loth must remain, with which the mercury is bound.

### Examination of the Sublimated Sulfur <note>original: *Examen sublimati sulphuris*</note>

When the matter has been worked on the fire for 7 or 8 hours, you must let the glass cool and then break it, remove the matter, and weigh it. If you find by the weight that there is still much more sulfur present than is appropriate, you must place the matter into another **vial** <term>Phiol</term>, similar to the previous one. Give it a gentle heat at first, and then for 4 or 6 hours a strong **flame-fire** <note>a direct, intense heat where the flames lick the vessel</note>, so that the superfluous sulfur may be **driven off** <note>literally "smelled out" (*abrieche*); the evaporation of volatile sulfurous fumes</note>. This allows the **mercury** <term>Mercurius</term> to be better **fixed** <term>figiret</term> <note>"Fixation" is the alchemical process of making a volatile substance stable under heat so it no longer evaporates</note>, so that it remains at the bottom in a hard **mass** <term>Massa</term>. It should be colored a reddish-grey and should not contain more than one **loth** <term>Loth</term> <note>a German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to half an ounce or 15 grams</note> of sulfur, or only slightly more.

S.</term> <note>original: *stratum super stratum*; a Latin phrase meaning "layer upon layer," referring to the alternating layers of different substances in a crucible</note>; then place the lid on the vessel and **seal it with luting** <term>lutiret</term> <note>the act of sealing a vessel with "lute," a specialized paste, to make it airtight and prevent the escape of vapors</note>. First Collection.

### Multiplication.

**Take** <note>original: "℞." (Recipe); a standard medical and alchemical abbreviation for "take"</note> one ounce, or as much as you wish, of your **prepared matter**. Grind it into a fine powder. Put it into a small flask or a vial. Warm it in sand or ash. Shake it in the glass. When the powder weighs one ounce, pour the fourth part—namely, **half a Loth** <term>Loth</term> <note>a traditional German unit of weight; in this period, 1 ounce usually equaled 2 *Loth*</note>—of **mercury** <note>original: "Mercurii"</note> onto it in the glass. Shake it well together. Place it back on the warm sand. Let it stand in the heat for half or a full quarter-hour. After that, lift it from the heat once more and shake it in the glass. Continue this for 4 or 5 hours; thus all the mercury will turn into a gray powder.

Then, let this powder stand in a warm **sand cupel** <term>Sandcapelle</term> <note>a vessel or enclosure containing sand to provide gentle, even heat to the glass flask</note> for 15 days and nights. After this, you may **augment** <term>Augmentation</term> <note>the process of increasing the quantity or potency of the alchemical substance</note> it again exactly as before, once more by a quarter-weight of the matter in the glass. Continue with this increase four, five, or six times, as you wish, depending on how far you want to bring it to a perfect **maturation** <note>original: "Zeitigung"; the alchemical concept of "ripening" the base metal into a noble one, like fruit ripening on a tree</note>.

After this augmentation, let it stand **by itself** <note>original: "per se"</note> in the warm sand for one month without any further addition of mercury; and for the last eight days, while still in the sand, provide a quite strong heat. Thus it will become a gray, **fixed** <term>fixres</term> <note>in alchemy, a "fixed" substance is stable under heat and no longer evaporates or changes state</note> powder. Melt this; then from 8 ounces of matter, you will find 5 to 6 parts or ounces of **true silver** <note>original: "gerechtes Silber"</note> on the cupel, sometimes a half

<summary>This page describes the Multiplication and Augmentation stages, where mercury is repeatedly added to the "prepared matter" and heated to increase the final yield of silver. Through a month-long heating process, the substance is "fixed" into a stable powder ready for smelting into pure silver.</summary>

<keywords>multiplication, augmentation, mercury, loth, maturation, fixed powder, silver, sand bath, alchemical smelting</keywords>

...half an ounce more or less, after you have diligently prepared the **mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"</note>.

It is also to be noted that during the **augmentation** <term>Augmentiren</term>, one should not pour the fourth part into the glass onto the powder all at once, but rather in three or four portions. One should also always shake the glass whenever a little has been added, so that the mercury gets among the powder. Then, leave the glass on the warm sand for about a quarter of an hour, shaking it frequently in the meantime; thus the matter mingles, and the powder swallows the mercury without delay.

\* Since in this process **mercury**, **sulfur**, and **salt** <note>original: "Mercurius, Sulphur und Sal"; the *Tria Prima* or three primary principles of alchemical theory</note> come together in coarse forms, many observations can be made regarding how a metal is gradually generated beneath the earth. In the Art <note>referring to the "Hermetic Art" or alchemy</note>, a transformation is the same as a **maturation** <term>Zeitigung</term> <note>the alchemical concept of "ripening" or "perfecting" a metal</note>, which occurs in a short time. In contrast, Nature requires a very long time until she brings a metal to maturity. Just as the mixing of the beginnings <note>the fundamental elements or principles</note> occurs, so also is the metal which the **central fire** <term>Centralfeuer</term> <note>the internal heat of the earth believed to drive geological and alchemical changes</note> of the earth cooks. This gray powder is an **ore** <term>Minera</term>. Only those with understanding know what kind of metal is hidden beneath the impure garments. The **silver** lets out a spirit, which the mercury draws to itself, and through sulfur and salt, retains it for its maturation into silver by means of the fire.

### 47.

## A Beautiful Growth of the Art.

<margin>Take <note>original: "Rx.", a standard symbol for a recipe or prescription</note></margin> A **strong water** <term>Aquafort</term> <note>original: "Aquafort," referring here to a type of nitric acid</note> made from **alum**, common salt, and **niter** <term>Nitro</term>, **of each equal parts** <note>original: "ana"</note>. In this, **dissolve** <note>original: "solvire"</note> one **ounce** <term>Loth</term> <note>A "Loth" was a historical unit of weight, roughly 14–17 grams</note> of fine silver, four ounces of purified mercury, and half a **drachm** <term>Quintlein</term> <note>A "Quintlein" was about 3.5 to 4 grams</note> of fine gold. Then, **dissolve** twice as much verdigris in another portion of **strong water**. Pour these two **solutions** together. Place them in a **vial** <note>original: "Phiole"</note> and set it in a **digestion** <term>Digestion</term> <note>a process of gentle, steady heating</note> or in the heat of the sun. Then, mountains with various growths will sprout within it. When these have grown up to the surface of the water, then pour the water off; the growth will then remain in its shape. However, if one allows them to grow out beyond the water, the whole structure will collapse together and look like lime <note>referring to a powdery residue or oxide</note>. Regarding the weight mentioned above, others take gold, silver, and **mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"</note> in equal parts.

* A greedy person seeks nothing but gold and silver from the Art <note>the "Hermetic Art" or alchemy</note>. But he who prizes wisdom higher than these metals delights in the contemplation of the omnipotence and wisdom of God in His visible works. The author of *The Opened Philosophical Father-Heart* <note>original: "eröfneten philosophischen Vaterherzens," likely referring to the 17th-century alchemical text *Der eröffnete philosophische Vater-Herze*</note> maintains that the possessor of his work is a little more than a king. Such a possessor does not compare himself to a king in power and glory, but rather in regards to wisdom and understanding—qualities which great lords claim for themselves with the title **Serene Highness** <term>Durchleucht</term>.

In this respect, a possessor of the **Great Work** <term>Great Work</term> <note>original: "grossen Werks," referring to the successful creation of the Philosopher's Stone</note> surpasses a king, since he can possess even in a single **tincture** <term>Tincture</term> <note>The "Tincture" or Elixir was the substance believed to transmute base metals into gold or heal all illnesses</note> a greater wealth than a king. There are, besides these, many other reasons why an **Adept** <term>Adeptus</term> <note>A master who has successfully completed the Great Work</note> can be something more than a king, especially when one considers the many frustrations that accompany the splendor of royal dignity. Since he is a king whom **God** <note>original: "GOtt"</note> has graced with such a great understanding of divine and natural secrets that his will has become a slave to his intellect; thus one can count an **artist** <note>In alchemy, the "Artist" is the practitioner of the "Hermetic Art"</note> who has reached this point among the spiritual kings, which Holy Scripture declares all children of God to be. 

Therefore, because of a glory that exalts the grace of God in such possessors, no resentment should be cast upon their arts; for through them they are **Magi** <term>Magi</term> <note>Wise men or philosopher-magicians; the term implies a deep understanding of the hidden laws of nature</note> who see beyond other men and distance themselves from the sins and vices to which most people, in order to earn their bread in the world, give free rein. As a Magus, an Adept is at the same time a **theologian** <term>Theologus</term>. He can be neither an atheist nor a **naturalist** <note>original: "Naturalist"; here referring to one who believes only in physical laws and denies the divine or supernatural</note>, since his intellect recognizes **Scripture** and **reason** as the rule of thought, and judges that these two eyes make a wise man. 

Such a Magus is not like that **Simon** <note>A reference to Simon Magus from the Bible (Acts 8), who attempted to purchase the power of the Holy Spirit, giving rise to the term "simony"</note>, intent on the means to acquire things that would bring him more frustration than pleasure; rather, he lives, as far as possible, in silence, and reflects on how he might promote the honor of God and the welfare of his neighbor with all the strength granted by God. He is a right **Freemason** <term>Freymäurer</term> <note>The author uses this term esoterically to mean one who builds a spiritual temple through "real secrets" rather than just ritual "appearance"</note> in real secrets, not in a mere appearance of them. Since he sees God daily through Scripture and Nature, he marvels at those who would rather see with one eye than with two,

### 48.

### *Hermetic Secret*, how to make Salmiac red and fixed.
<note>Salmiac is ammonium chloride, a salt used in alchemy to help dissolve metals or "fix" volatile substances so they can withstand heat.</note>

Take one ounce of vitriol <term>Vitriol</term> <note>A sulfate of metal; here, likely iron sulfate heated until it turns red</note> calcined to redness, and one ounce of salmiac, **of each** <note>original Latin: "ana"</note>. Grind them together and **soak** <note>original Latin: "imbibire"; the process of saturating a dry powder with liquid</note> them with distilled **urine**. Afterward, dry it, grind it thoroughly, and **sublime** <note>original Latin: "sublimire"; the process of heating a substance until it vapors and then collects again as a solid</note> it three times over the **residue** <note>original Latin: "feces"; the dregs or solid remains left at the bottom of the vessel</note>. Keep this safe.

Take one ounce of red calcined vitriol and **dissolve** <note>original Latin: "solvire"</note> it in distilled **urine**. **Filter** this **solution**.

Take one ounce of quicklime <term>Quicklime</term> <note>Calcium oxide, a caustic substance produced by heating limestone</note> and one ounce of the salmiac that was **prepared** above. Rub them together and, by means of **soaking** it with the red **urine** mentioned above, make a dough from it, and from that, an icy cake. Then take a glazed pot. Fill it to a quarter of its capacity with quicklime. Place the aforementioned cake upon this lime, with which the pot must then be completely filled, after you have firmly pressed down the bottom lime

Place it for two days in a potter's kiln. Then take it out. Break up the "cake" <note>the hardened mass of chemicals</note> thoroughly and wash it with warm water until the saltiness has been removed. ### A water which dissolves all metals and precious stones, indeed all things in the world.

Mix these three items. Then take a dried **vitriol** <note>iron or copper sulfate</note> and saltpeter, **of each** <note>original Latin: "ana"</note>. **Saturate** <term>Imbibire</term> <note>The process of soaking a dry substance with liquid until it is fully absorbed.</note> these with the above-mentioned waters, which you then **distill off** <term>abstrahirest</term>. Set the oil aside, and saturate with it again a dried vitriol and a saltpeter, of which there should be just as much. Distill, and repeat the distillation,

### 50.

## Of the Fire of the Adepts.

The "fire" of the adepts <note>In alchemy, "fire" does not always mean a flame; it often refers to corrosive acids or "potential fires" that can dissolve or transform matter.</note> is of such secret excellence that its power cannot yet be fully explained. It is of various kinds, and one is made as follows:

<term>Take:</term> <term>Mercury sublimate</term> <note>original Latin: "Mercurium sublimatum cum vitriolo & sale communi"; Mercury(II) chloride prepared with vitriol (sulfates) and common salt.</note> with vitriol and common salt. Its **quintessence** <note>The "fifth essence," or the most purified and potent form of a substance.</note>, however, would be even better. Furthermore, take **sal ammoniac** <term>Salmiac</term> which has been sublimated <note>Purified by heating the solid until it becomes a vapor and then collects as a solid again.</note> nine or ten times. Mix and grind them diligently. Place it upon a marble stone. Set it out at night in the calm and clear air, or in a damp cellar; thus it will be changed into such a powerful water that, if even the smallest drop falls upon a hand, it immediately bores a hole through it. It does the same to a sheet of copper or iron. But when silver, tin, lead, or copper are placed in this "fire," it transforms these metals into the likeness of a small pearl.

* Among the secret fires of the artists <note>The term "artists" here refers to practitioners of alchemy and the chemical arts.</note> are those with which the metals are fundamentally <term>radicaliter</term> dissolved <term>solviret</term>. One comes down from above, and the other comes up from below, as there are only two ways which have preference over all others and shall remain the property of the Wise, especially the one whose fire comes to us from above. Here, we wish to further reveal that he who wants to work successfully must first destroy and calcine <term>calciniren</term> <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or "ash."</note> the seven metals and the two or three most noble minerals, turning them into a lime <term>Kalk</term> or "crocus" <term>Croco</term> <note>A reddish-colored powder, usually a metallic oxide, named for its resemblance to the color of saffron.</note> using a dissolving water <term>Solvirwasser</term> or extraction water <term>Extrahirwasser</term>. From these metallic and mineral limes <term>Calcibus</term> or powders <term>Crocis</term>, one must then produce a living Mercury <term>Mercurium</term> by means of liquid salt of tartar <term>liquorischen Weinsteinsalzes</term> and sal ammoniac <term>Salmiacs</term>. This living Mercury <term>Mercurius</term> of the metals and minerals is further changed back into a lime <term>Calcem</term> and sublimate <term>Sublimat</term> through a dissolving spirit <term>Spiritum</term> and by sublimating <term>Sublimiren</term>. From this sublimate <term>Sublimat</term> of the metals and minerals, one then creates a liquid <term>Liquorem</term> by mixing it with sal ammoniac <term>Salmiacs</term> and letting it flow by liquefaction <term>per deliquium</term> <note>The process where a solid salt absorbs moisture from the air until it dissolves into a liquid state.</note> in a cellar or other damp place. When this liquid <term>Liquor</term>, by distilling <term>destillando</term>, has become a mercurial spirit <term>Spiritus</term>, it is then a universal key <term>Universalschlüssel</term> to all metallic and mineral things, used to extract their noblest sulfur <term>Sulphur</term> <note>In alchemy, "sulfur" refers to the active, oily, and combustible principle or the "soul" of a substance.</note> and to be used further—namely, for particular and universal processes <term>Particular- und Universalprocessen</term>. Through this sulfur <term>Sulphur</term>, one must [bring] the vegetable, animal, and mineral solvents <term>Menstrua</term> to a—
